# Known limits and trade-offs

Every limit of Tracekit is written down next to the feature it belongs to, which makes it easy to miss when you read
only one page. This page collects them in one place. Each release's notes link here, and a doc that adds a "Limits"
section must be linked from this page (`tests/test_docs.py` checks it).

Each row says what the limit is, why it is so, what to do about it, and where the details are. Rows marked
**(1.1)** describe behaviour new in 1.1, not in 1.0; **(1.2)**, new in 1.2; **(1.3)**, new in 1.3; **(1.4)**, new in 1.4.

What Tracekit guarantees and doesn't, per deployment, on one page: [guarantees](guarantees.md).

## Evidence and verification

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| A verified bundle doesn't prove intent. | A record says a call was made, not why. | Read decisions, approvals and reasons alongside the records. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove) |
| A verified bundle doesn't prove that everything the agent did was recorded. | An action that crosses no capture layer (a subprocess, code that skips the adapter, a call around a gateway that isn't mandatory) leaves no record. **(1.2)** What some layer saw and nothing reported is now a signed gap: a call allowed or approved that never completed (`decided_not_completed`), a tool result the gateway saw go back with no decide (`reconcile.hook_missing`, at once), client counter values never received (`client_counter_gap`, also at `close_run`), writes after `run.final` (`late_activity`), a second run from inside a bound harness process (`concurrent_run`), a tailer that stops (`tailer_lost`) and a second tailer token for a run (`tailer_redelegated`). A call no layer saw still leaves nothing. | Read the `coverage`, `tiers` and gap lines; add an out-of-process layer (the [LLM gateway](gateway.md), the [tool executor](executor.md), the [MCP gateway](mcp-gateway.md)). | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove), [verdicts](verdicts.md#informational-lines-always-pass) |
| A verified bundle doesn't prove that a tool result the agent reported is true. | In-process capture (T1) records what the agent's process reported; only out-of-process capture (T2) sees what crossed a boundary. | Prefer T2 evidence for calls that matter; use `executor: t2` approvals. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove), [approvals](approvals.md#tiers-t1-and-t2-executors) |
| A `tool.result`'s `output.size` is the size of what the signer received. For a tool whose result the adapters send as its digest (every tool the policy doesn't list as untrusted), that is the digest's size, not the result's. | The signer records what it can check; a size the client claimed would be the agent's word. | Read `output.size` as the size of the content committed to; list a tool under `untrusted:` to have its result itself sent, redacted and committed to. | [privacy](privacy.md) |
| Imported OpenTelemetry (T3) proves only that spans were received. | Spans are exported after the work is done, by the application. | Gate calls with an adapter or hook; treat T3 runs as a log, not evidence of completeness. | [OpenTelemetry](otel.md#receiving-traces-tier-t3-import) |
| agentgateway's MCP spans import without arguments or results, and each request is its own trace, so its own run, unless the agent sends `traceparent`. | agentgateway's spans name the tool and its outcome; the import keys runs by trace id. | Add `gen_ai.tool.call.arguments` and `gen_ai.tool.call.result` through agentgateway's tracing `attributes`; send `traceparent` from the agent; for evidence of what ran, use the `ext_mcp` processor. | [agentgateway](integrations/agentgateway.md#the-otlp-import) |
| An imported run closes when its trace's root span arrives; a trace whose root never reaches the signer (its root is another process's, as with `traceparent` through agentgateway) stays open until `idle_s` and counts against the importer's `open_runs` (100): past it every batch is refused (`503`, retried) until runs idle out. A span that ends after its root and comes more than `grace_s` behind it is rejected. | The import can't tell a trace is done but by its root; open runs are held in memory. | Export the root span to the signer too, raise the importer's `open_runs` in `limits`, or lower `idle_s`. | [OpenTelemetry](otel.md#receiving-traces-tier-t3-import) |
| Tracekit is tamper-evident, not tamper-proof: a deletion shows, it isn't prevented. | Prevention would need attested hardware (a TEE), which Tracekit doesn't claim. | Keep independent witnesses and a monitor so a rewrite is detected. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-cant-tracekit-prove) |
| Whoever holds the signer's key can rewrite or fork its log; nothing in the bundle alone shows it. | Signatures prove who signed, not that the key holder didn't sign again. | Pin a witness the operator doesn't run (`witnessed`) and a monitor (`witnessed+monitored`). | [server threat model](threat-model-server.md#s4-the-operator-organisation-and-the-exporter) |
| Records after the last cosigned checkpoint are unproven. | A witness vouches only for what it has cosigned. | Export after the next checkpoint; read the `log tail` warning. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#warnings---strict-exits-3), [witnesses](witnesses.md#trust-model-per-witness-type) |
| A bundle exported before its run is finalised verifies only `TO HEAD n (open)`. | The signer finalises a run a few seconds after it closes. | Re-export after `run.final`; for audits require `VERIFIED`. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#export-and-verify-by-hand), [verdicts](verdicts.md#integrity) |
| **(1.4)** A run cut back (records or parts dropped from its end, with an inclusion proof of the record now last) verifies `TO HEAD n (open)`, as an export before its end does: nothing shows that records were dropped. | The proofs file isn't signed, and from a run's records an inclusion proof of any of them can often be computed; only the signer's `run.final` or a run-set says where a run ends. | For audits require `VERIFIED` (the run with its `run.final`), or a run-set with `run-set: COMPLETE`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout), [verdicts](verdicts.md#integrity) |
| A single run's bundle doesn't show that no other run was left out. | The exporter chooses which runs to export. | Ask for the tenant's run-set bundle and require `run-set: COMPLETE`. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md#3-get-the-bundles) |
| **(1.4)** `tracekit export --v2 --run-set` always starts at registry size 0 and bundles every finished run of the tenant, one file each: past about 10,000 finished runs (the format's 10,000 files) or 512 MiB in all it refuses, saying which limit it is over. | The command has no later start, and the format caps a bundle's files and size so that verifying it needs bounded memory. | Give each workload or team its own tenant, or start a new `data_dir` (a new log) before a tenant nears the limit. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md#3-get-the-bundles) |
| A withheld key retirement isn't visible unless a run-set from registry size 0 reaches the bundle. A bundle whose key records show one key and no retirement says so only on its `Assurance` line, not as a warning: a retirement withheld together with its new key would look the same. | Only the registry log proves which `key.retire` records exist. | Read `key retirements not proven complete` on the `Assurance` line (a `keys` warning when the key records show a retired key); require a run-set from registry size 0 where it matters. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#warnings---strict-exits-3) |
| `key assurance` is `asserted` unless a record-key issuer certifies the keys. | Without an issuer the log declares its own record keys. | Run an [issuer](issuer.md) and pin it in the trust config. | [issuer](issuer.md#verifying) |
| The verifier doesn't fetch revocations. | Verification is offline and takes trust only from the verifier's side. | Pass the issuer's log with `--revocations`. | [issuer](issuer.md#limits) |
| A trust config the operator supplies proves nothing about the operator. | The bundle carries no code or trust (I9); whoever writes the trust config decides what verifies. | Build `trust.json` from independent sources and install the verifier yourself. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md#2-build-the-trust-config-from-independent-sources) |
| On a pull request the GitHub Action reads a `trust:` file, a `key:` file and a `witness: file:` path in the pull request's checkout from its base commit, and refuses one in `$GITHUB_WORKSPACE` that it can't: not at the base commit, given with `..`, through a symlink, in a submodule, or in no git checkout there. But on `pull_request` GitHub runs the workflow file as the pull request has it, so its author can still change the step's inputs or drop the step. | The action can change where its inputs come from, not the workflow that passes them. | Make the verify job a required workflow in a repository ruleset (it runs from the branch you name), or have CODEOWNERS review `.github/workflows/`; keep files the workflow writes itself in `$RUNNER_TEMP`. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#verify-in-github-actions) |
| A v1 bundle verified without `--key` or `--witness` is only internally consistent (`UNANCHORED`). | A bundle carries its own public key, so a bundle forged with a fresh key is consistent too. | Pin the signer's key or an independent witness. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#trust-roots-for-verification) |
| v1: the exporter can replace a selected record with an elided stub, and `manifest.json` is unsigned. | v1 stubs are not bound to a run; the manifest is an index, recomputed from signed records. | Use v2 bundles; read the "run completeness unproven" warning. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#open-problems) |
| Record timestamps come from the signer host's clock; `seq` and the chain prove order only. | Only witnesses and RFC 3161 timestamps give independent time. | Read cosignature and anchor times in the `Assurance` line. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#what-each-attack-comes-to), [witnesses](witnesses.md#v2-rekor-v2-anchors-and-rfc-3161-timestamps) |
| A v1 record after a v1 → v2 bridge is reported only with `--v1-ledger`. | The frozen v1 verifier sees bridge records as ordinary gaps. | Verify bridged logs with `--v1-ledger`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#12-v1--v2-bridge) |
| The TypeScript verifier reports `UNVERIFIABLE` for bundles that rely on a Rekor anchor, an SLH-DSA checkpoint signature or certified record keys. | Web Crypto has no SLH-DSA, and those checks aren't ported yet; it never says `VERIFIED` for a check it skipped. | Verify such bundles with the Python verifier (`pip install tracekit-ai`). | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md), [format v2](format-v2.md#13-implementations) |
| A verifier doesn't check what an ext name it doesn't interpret says: the records verify (signature, chain, inclusion), and each such name is a `not interpreted` warning. | Format 1.4's non-critical slot lets a later Tracekit add what an older verifier can leave unread, instead of locking that verifier out. | Verify with the version the warning names (`tracekit >= x checks it`); `--strict` stops on it. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout), [verdicts](verdicts.md#warnings---strict-exits-3) |
| A 1.3 or older verifier reports a bundle with an `ext` field or record `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.4.0)` and prints no commands to verify it. | The slot and the upgrade lines are new in 1.4.0. | Run the commands in [verdicts](verdicts.md#a-bundle-that-needs-a-newer-verifier). | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| The browser verifier (`verify.html`) is the TypeScript verifier: it reports `UNVERIFIABLE` where that one does (SLH-DSA, Rekor anchors, certified record keys, `tracekit why` findings) and takes no monitor reports, revocations or v1 ledger; a v1 bundle it names the command for. Without a trust file it is never `VERIFIED`. In a browser that refuses its worker it verifies in the page itself, which a bundle of 100k records holds for seconds. A copy someone else hosts is only as good as its bytes. | It runs on Web Crypto in the page; a browser can't check where a page came from. | Use `tracekit verify` for those checks and for very large bundles in such a browser; get the trust file from the sender; for evidence, open a copy you checked with `gh attestation verify verify.html --repo Cygnux-Labs/Tracekit`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#13-implementations), [releasing](RELEASING.md#verifying-a-release) |
| The browser verifier holds the bundle, unzipped, and every record it parses in the tab's memory, about 8 times the bundle unzipped: a run's 100,000 records (50,000 tool calls: a 28 MB bundle, 130 MiB unzipped in three parts) take about 14 s in Chrome, and the same verifier as a CLI 1 GB ([E22](evaluation.md#e22-verifier-agreement-on-real-bundles)). A bundle near the format's 512 MiB, some 390,000 records, would need about 4 GB, more than a tab may hold: it can close the tab. Without a trust file the page reads every record, since no pinned key stops a bundle at its checkpoint. | It verifies offline, in the page, under the format's limits (512 MiB unzipped); a line shorter than a signed record is refused before any is parsed, so a small file can't unzip into millions of records. | Choose the sender's trust file: a bundle their signer didn't sign then fails at its checkpoint before a record is read. Verify very large bundles with `tracekit verify` or `npx @cygnuxlabs/tracekit verify`. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| A run's records must fit one bundle: 512 MiB unzipped, about 390,000 records (some 190,000 tool calls with small arguments, fewer with larger ones). **(1.4)** A run past 64 MiB of records (about 24,000 tool calls) is written in parts of up to 64 MiB, which a 1.3 or older verifier can't read: it reports the bundle `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.4.0)`. `tracekit export` refuses a bundle of more than 512 MiB in all or 10,000 files, and says which limit it is over. Both command-line verifiers hold about 8 times the bundle unzipped in memory: 1 GB for 50,000 tool calls, some 4 GB at the limit. | The format caps each file of a bundle at 64 MiB and the whole at 512 MiB, so verifying one needs bounded memory; a bundle past them is `UNUSABLE BUNDLE` to every verifier. | Verify a bundle with parts with tracekit 1.4 or newer (Python, TypeScript or the page), on a machine with 4 GB free for the largest. Start a new run for each task or session; a run still open exports up to its head, so export a very long one before it reaches the limit. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| A run with 20 problems (bad signatures, schema errors and broken chain links together) is checked no further: the report names those 20 and how many of the run's records after them went unchecked, so a run with one record deleted early lists 20 broken links, not one per later record. Every record is still parsed, so such a bundle holds memory as any of its size. | A bundle of forged records (one signed record repeated, or a chain rebuilt without the key) would otherwise cost a signature check per record: a 1.4 MB file that unzips to 256 MiB of repeated records took 103 s in Python and 39 s in TypeScript on one laptop; now 7 s and 6 s. | The bundle has `FAILED` already: ask the sender for a fresh export from the signer, and read its first problems for what was changed. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#integrity-checks-a-fail-here-fails-the-bundle) |
| The TypeScript verifier needs Node 20.12 or newer (or a current browser). | It uses JSON imports and `DecompressionStream("deflate-raw")`, which earlier Node 20 releases lack. | Upgrade Node, or use the Python verifier. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| `npx @cygnuxlabs/tracekit verify` with `--monitor-report`, `--revocations` or the v1 bridge options runs the Python verifier, which must be installed. | Only the Python verifier has those checks. | `pip install tracekit-ai`, or verify without those options. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md) |
| **(1.2)** A bundle holding a run started by another run (`parent_run`, a sub-agent's run) needs tracekit 1.2 or newer; a 1.1 verifier reports it `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.2.0)`. | Every v2 record definition is closed, so an older verifier can't read the new fields; the manifest's `verifier_min_version` makes it say so instead of `FAILED`. | Verify with tracekit 1.2 or newer (Python or TypeScript). | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| **(1.2)** A bundle holding a 1.2 completeness gap (`decided_not_completed`, `late_activity`, `concurrent_run`, `tailer_redelegated`, a gap's `run_id`), a `run.child` record or a run.final with `provider_executed` needs tracekit 1.2 or newer; older verifiers report it `UNVERIFIABLE (needs tracekit >= 1.2.0)`. | The new kinds and fields are outside the 1.1 schema; the manifest's `verifier_min_version` says so. | Verify with tracekit 1.2 or newer. | [format v2](format-v2.md#9-bundle-layout) |
| Experimental surfaces may change between releases and get less hardening than the stable core. | Few people run them yet; the stable core is what is tested end to end and fixed first. | Build on the stable core; each experimental command or server says so on stderr when it starts. | [what's stable](support.md) |
| JSON nested deeper than 128 levels is refused: a bundle file is unusable, a record fails, and a tool call's arguments are denied (`TK-ARGS-INVALID`). | A fixed depth makes every verifier and the signer read the same documents the same way, whatever their parser's limits. | Flatten arguments deeper than that. | [format v2](format-v2.md#1-canonical-json) |

## Dev mode vs system mode

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| **v1 tracekitd:** witness publishing (git push, HTTP) runs under the signer's lock, so an unreachable witness stalls appends for up to 15–60 s; a local user can hold the 64 connections by trickling bytes (the dev socket is world-writable); `runs.json` is rewritten on every append. | The v1 daemon is single-writer and laptop-sized. | Use the v2 signer for anything shared or busy; keep v1 witnesses reachable. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md) |
| **v1 tracekitd:** a restart takes every run of the ledger as active again, so past the run cap it writes `run_state_evicted` gaps for long-finished runs, and a run that never sent `run.start` loses its owner. Parallel hooks of one session can record a false `trace.tamper` (transcript length read before the send). Client state files (`~/.tracekit-client/runs/`, Gemini call ids, transcript acks) are never pruned. | v1 state is rebuilt from the ledger and kept per laptop. | Read those gaps as restart artefacts; prune `~/.tracekit-client/runs/` by hand. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md) |
| Same-user dev mode protects nothing against the agent itself. | The dev signer runs as your user, so an agent that runs commands as you can read its key, stop it, or rewrite and re-sign its store. Policy rules (`TK-D007`, `TK-D013`) are tripwires. | Use system mode, a sidecar or a central signer for anything you need to rely on; dev bundles say `isolation: same-user`. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-does-same-user-dev-mode-protect), [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#what-dev-assurance-means) |
| `Assurance` describes checkpoint cosigning, not isolation: a same-user signer with a witness verifies as `witnessed`. | The two are independent properties. | Read `isolation` as well as `Assurance`. | [verdicts](verdicts.md#assurance), [witnesses](witnesses.md#v2-c2sp-tlog-witnesses) |
| A system-mode signer without a witness verifies as `Assurance: dev`. | Nothing outside the host shows the log wasn't rolled back. | Add a witness on another host that the operator doesn't run. | [deployment](deploy.md#what-decides-a-bundles-assurance) |
| System mode doesn't protect against root on the machine. | Root can read the key and rewrite the store. | Witnesses on another host detect a rewrite of what they cosigned. | [deployment](deploy.md#laptop-system-mode) |
| In system mode without `--managed`, the agent can remove the hook from its own `~/.claude/settings.json` (or from its other coding agent's config), with no record and no gap. With or without it, the agent can act outside its coding agent, where no hook runs. | Without `--managed` the hook lives in the agent user's own settings. `--managed` is wired for Claude Code only: Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, Qwen Code, OpenCode, goose, Factory Droid and Antigravity CLI keep their hooks in the agent user's config (init refuses `--managed` with their `--agent`). | For Claude Code, `init --v2 --user AGENT --managed` (v1: `init --managed`): the hooks go in Claude Code's admin-managed settings, which Claude Code's docs say no user, project or local setting and no `--settings` overrides, and whose hooks a `disableAllHooks` outside managed settings can't turn off. Outside the hooks, an out-of-process record: the gateways, or `--egress`. | [deployment](deploy.md#admin-managed-hooks-claude-code) |
| **(1.5.1)** `--managed` hooks run in every user's Claude Code on the machine, not only the agent's: other users' calls are recorded (the approver's in the agent's tenant, anyone else's in `local`), decided by the policy and blocked while the signer is down, and a call the policy holds in their sessions can't be approved (the approver may not answer their own run's, and sees no other tenant's). `--managed-only` also stops every user's own Claude Code hooks, and what init writes beside the hooks applies to every user too: `TRACEKIT_SIGNER`, with `--egress` the `HTTPS_PROXY` and `HTTP_PROXY` that send their Claude Code's traffic through the egress observer, and the thinking summaries init turns on (`showThinkingSummaries`). | Claude Code's managed settings apply to the whole machine; the hook doesn't skip other users. | Use `--managed` on a host or VM where the agent's user is the only one running Claude Code; on a laptop you also use Claude Code on, init without `--managed`. | [deployment](deploy.md#admin-managed-hooks-claude-code) |
| **(1.5.1)** Claude Code applies one managed source by default: a server-managed (claude.ai console or gateway) or MDM policy that sets any key replaces `managed-settings.json`, Tracekit's hooks with it, and doctor sees neither. Claude Code fetches server-managed settings when the session signs in to Anthropic with an eligible credential, so which account the agent signs in with counts too. | Claude Code's `managedSourcesBehavior` defaults to `"first-wins"` ([Claude Code docs](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings#how-claude-code-combines-managed-sources)). | In the agent's Claude Code, `/status` must list `Enterprise managed settings (file)`. Where your organization delivers server-managed or MDM settings, add Tracekit's hooks there or set `managedSourcesBehavior: "merge"` in the highest one; pin the sign-in with `forceLoginOrgUUID` in `managed-settings.json` (Claude Code takes sign-in keys from the file ahead of server-managed settings). | [deployment](deploy.md#admin-managed-hooks-claude-code) |
| **(1.5.1)** When `managed-settings.json` isn't valid JSON, Tracekit can't take its hooks out of it. If `/etc/tracekit/client.json` records the file (the last init used `--managed`), `init --v2`, with or without `--managed` or `--no-hooks`, and `uninstall --v2` stop with an error before they change anything. If it doesn't, they leave the file as it is, Tracekit's hooks in it included (`init --v2 --managed` stops with an error before it changes any hooks): once an admin fixes the file, those hooks run again, against whatever the config then names. | The file holds an admin's other settings, which init won't rewrite from a file it can't parse. Init checks the recorded file before it writes anything, so a stopped init leaves no hooks the config doesn't record. | Fix the file, then run `tracekit init --v2 --user <agent> --managed` (or `tracekit uninstall --v2`) again, and check `tracekit doctor`. | [deployment](deploy.md#admin-managed-hooks-claude-code) |
| **(1.5.1)** Claude Code's docs don't say whether `claude --bare` (or `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1`), which skips "auto-discovery of hooks", runs managed hooks; they do say `--safe-mode` runs them. Doctor reads `managed-settings.json` only, not `managed-settings.d/` drop-ins, so a `disableAllHooks` an admin puts in a drop-in turns the hooks off unseen. An admin of the machine can edit the managed file. | Not documented by Claude Code; drop-ins aren't read yet. | As the agent's user, run `claude --bare -p "run ls"` and check a run is recorded before you rely on `--managed`; with `--egress` (Linux) the agent's outside connections are recorded whether or not a hook runs. Keep `disableAllHooks` out of the drop-ins. The agent's user must not be an admin (init refuses one). | [deployment](deploy.md#admin-managed-hooks-claude-code), [egress](egress.md) |
| v1 system mode (init as root without `--v2`) has no uninstall: `tracekit uninstall` takes the hooks out of user and project settings only, not out of admin-managed settings (`--managed`), and leaves tracekitd's service, `/var/lib/tracekit`, `/opt/tracekit` and `/etc/tracekit/client.json`. | v1 system mode is the legacy path; `sudo /opt/tracekit/bin/python -I -m tracekit uninstall --v2` removes v2 system mode, `--managed` hooks included. | Remove Tracekit's hook groups (and `allowManagedHooksOnly`, if init set it) from the managed settings file, stop and remove the tracekitd unit (or launchd plist), then those paths. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#system-mode-linux-macos-experimental) |
| Without harness binding, any process of the agent's user can write into the agent's runs or start runs of its own. | The signer knows the caller by uid; run tokens are readable by that uid. | Register the harness (`--harness`, Linux). | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#system-mode-linux-macos-experimental) |
| System mode requires an agent user that is not root and not in a sudo, wheel, admin, docker or similar group. | Such a user could take over the signer. | `tracekit doctor` (`D-AGENT-PRIV`) checks it. | [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| When the transcript tailer's ACL or sudo rule can't be set up, every run records a `tailer_lost` gap instead. | The tailer needs read access to the agent's transcripts. | Fix the setup init reports. | [deployment](deploy.md#laptop-system-mode) |

## Signer and capture

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| A tool class set to fail `open` runs unrecorded while the signer is down. | It is the setting you choose when an outage must not stop the agent; the next call writes a signed gap for exactly the missed calls. | Keep `fail_modes` closed (the default); the report warns on every fail-open class. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-happens-when-the-signer-is-down) |
| While the signer is unreachable, the LangChain adapter, the Claude Code hook and the Hermes Agent plugin pick a call's fail mode by the class the shipped default policy gives the tool (`terminal` is `shell`, an unmapped tool `unknown`); the MCP and Browser Use adapters by their fixed class (`mcp`, `browser`); the OpenAI Agents, Claude Agent SDK and CrewAI adapters apply `default` to every tool. A signer whose policy maps the tool to another class may have another fail mode for it. | The tool's class is the signer's decision, and the signer can't be asked. | Keep fail modes closed for classes your policy remaps, or set `default`; with OpenAI Agents, the Claude Agent SDK or CrewAI, set `default`. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-happens-when-the-signer-is-down) |
| Model calls (autotrace) while the signer is down: under a closed `model` fail mode a request that can't be recorded is refused (`PermissionError`, saying so and what to do); a response the signer missed (it went down mid-call) is sent again at the next model call or at exit, after a signed `client_counter_gap` for the try that failed. A signer that stays down until the process exits leaves the request without its response, and the run open until the signer's idle timeout. | The client records only while the signer answers, and keeps missed responses in memory (the newest 1024). | Keep the signer running (system mode restarts it); set `fail_modes: {model: open}` to let model calls run unrecorded during an outage. | [FAQ](faq.md#what-happens-when-the-signer-is-down) |
| v1 hooks allow a call when the payload can't be parsed (Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, goose, Qwen Code, Factory Droid, Crush, Devin and CodeBuddy Code, whose v1 hook commands don't name their event: under the default fail mode, open) or the harness is unknown; a payload nested too deep to read blocks the call. The v1 OpenCode plugin and Antigravity CLI's v1 hooks name their event, so a pre-tool call their hook can't decide is blocked whatever the fail mode. **(1.2)** v2 hooks that `tracekit init` wrote from 1.2 on name their event, so a pre-tool payload that can't be parsed is blocked; a post-tool one is reported on stderr, and the call it ends is completed as an error at the session's next Stop, prompt or end. v2 hooks installed before 1.2 still let an unparseable payload through, unrecorded; `tracekit doctor` (`D-HOOKS-EVENT`) finds event-less commands for Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, goose, Qwen Code, Factory Droid, Antigravity CLI and Crush. | In v1 a Tracekit failure follows the fail mode. A hook that can't parse its payload knows its event only from the command line. Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Gemini CLI write the payload themselves; OpenCode's plugin passes the model's arguments before OpenCode checks them. Doctor checks those command hooks, goose's, Qwen Code's, Factory Droid's, Antigravity CLI's and Crush's, but not OpenCode's JavaScript plugin. | Re-run `tracekit init` after upgrading (`tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent NAME` repairs the other coding agents' hooks); in v1 set `TRACEKIT_FAIL_CLOSED=1` or `fail_mode: closed`. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#hooks-name-their-event), [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| A v1 dev hook that can't start (its Python moved or was uninstalled, or Tracekit no longer imports) lets the pre-tool call run unrecorded, with an error the coding agent shows, even under the policy's `fail_mode: closed`, unless `TRACEKIT_FAIL_CLOSED=1` is set; on Windows, even then. goose (`on_failure: block`), OpenCode, Antigravity CLI, a Cursor hook init wrote with `failClosed: true` and system mode (unless its config sets `fail_mode: open`) block it. | The hook's command can read an environment variable, but the fail mode in the policy only Tracekit's hook reads; on Windows there is no POSIX shell to wrap the command in. | With `fail_mode: closed`, also set `TRACEKIT_FAIL_CLOSED=1` where the coding agent runs (Claude Code: under `env` in its settings), or use the v2 hooks (`tracekit init --dev --v2`), whose hook that can't start blocks the call. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| v1 hooks don't hold a Codex patch applied through the shell (`TK-A034` is in the v2 packs); v1 and v2 hooks both decide every file an `apply_patch` call adds, updates, deletes or moves to. | The v1 policy is kept as it was; `TK-A034` is a v2 rule. | Use the v2 hooks (`tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent codex`). | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#on-the-v2-signer) |
| The signer can't tell a real event from a well-formed fake sent by the agent's identity. | Anything that can call the signer as the agent can submit plausible events. | Cross-check with an independent layer: the gateway (`gateway_mandatory`) or, in v1, the model proxy. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#two-properties-kept-apart), [signing](signing.md#what-the-signer-checks-before-signing) |

| Capture layer L5 (services) is not shipped, and L4 (processes) only as the experimental [kernel sensor import](integrations/tetragon.md) (Linux): a sensor that covered a run throughout makes its processes `observed`, and its effects are the sensor's word, not reconciled. **(1.2)** L6 (network) is the [egress observer](egress.md), hosts only. | Out-of-process capture of services isn't built yet; the coverage matrix says `not covered` for them, and for processes where no sensor covered the run. | On Linux, import a kernel sensor ([Tetragon](integrations/tetragon.md), [Falco](integrations/falco.md)); use the [LLM gateway](gateway.md), the executor and the egress observer as the other out-of-process records. | [server threat model](threat-model-server.md#known-limits) |
| SDK and adapter capture is opt-in per call: only wrapped calls are seen and gated. | An adapter sees only the calls routed through it. | Wrap every tool; each integration lists what it doesn't cover. | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#two-properties-kept-apart), [OpenAI Agents](integrations/openai-agents.md#not-covered), [MCP](integrations/mcp.md#not-covered), [Browser Use](integrations/browser-use.md#not-covered), [Claude Agent SDK](integrations/claude-agent-sdk.md#not-covered), [CrewAI](integrations/crewai.md#not-covered), [Pydantic AI](integrations/pydantic-ai.md#not-covered), [Google ADK](integrations/google-adk.md#not-covered), [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered), [LlamaIndex](integrations/llamaindex.md#not-covered), [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered), [Strands](integrations/strands.md#not-covered), [Hermes Agent](integrations/hermes.md#not-covered) |

| **(1.2)** `decided_not_completed` is written at `run.final` only, and doesn't say why the result never came (the agent stopped or crashed, its post-call hook didn't run, or a client dropped it). A decision is also exempt when a later decision of the same call completed, except one whose approval was consumed and whose call didn't complete with the approved arguments; only the call's latest decision counts as later, so an open decision before a completed one and an open one after it is a gap too. | The signer sees the decide and the missing `complete`, not the agent or the harness's prompt. At most 100 per run are listed, then one gap counts the rest. | Read the gap next to the run's other records and the transcript or app log; a crash usually ends the run early too. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-the-signer-records-as-a-gap) |
| **(1.2)** A coding-agent hook completes each call that got no post-tool event (declined in the harness's own permission prompt, interrupted, or a post-tool payload it couldn't read) as an error when the session ends. That result is the hook's word, not the harness's; a session killed before its end event leaves such calls as `decided_not_completed` gaps instead. | No hook event says that a call was declined or interrupted, and a background sub-agent's call can still run when a turn ends; the session's end is the first point where none can. | Read a `tool.result` error "no PostToolUse came" as "not seen to run". | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-the-signer-records-as-a-gap) |

A prompt, Stop, tool result or session event whose hook fails (the signer unreachable, `tracekit` missing or broken) runs unrecorded, with a hook error in the transcript. Hook commands `tracekit init` wrote before 1.3, and Cursor hooks it wrote up to 1.4.2 (`failClosed` on every event), still turn such a failure into a block. | On a prompt, a block erases it; on Stop, it sends the agent back to work in a loop. Every tool call is still decided before it runs. | Read hook errors in the transcript; run `tracekit init` again after upgrading to get the current commands (`tracekit doctor` flags old ones in dev and system mode as `D-HOOKS-EVENT`, and old system-mode ones also as `D-HOOKS-VENV`). | [plugin](../plugin/README.md#when-a-hook-cant-run) |
| **(1.2)** `concurrent_run` covers only runs bound to a harness process (harness binding, Linux system mode): a second run started from inside the same harness process while another session's run is open there, once both have recorded an event since it registered. SDK and server runs, which run several at once by design, and harnesses without binding get no such check. A run started with `parent` (a sub-agent's) gets none either: the agent can read its session's run token, so a second session can pose as a child, and shows only as a `run.child` record in the parent. Which runs overlap is kept in memory and snapshots: a signer restart with no snapshot since forgets a pending one. | One harness process drives one session at a time; without a binding the signer can't tell a forged second run from a legitimate parallel one, and an older session's run left open and idle is no overlap. | Turn on harness binding for coding agents; pass `parent` for a sub-agent's run; read each `run.child` against the harness's transcripts. | [format v2](format-v2.md#3-events) |
| **(1.2)** A killed transcript tailer is a `tailer_lost` gap only in system mode, once it has made no call (heartbeat or record) for 90 s while the run is open, counted from when the hook took its token, and only while the signer runs: the watch is kept in memory. In dev mode the tailer runs as the agent's user, which may not send heartbeats, so a killed tailer leaves no gap. Heartbeats show that a process holding the run's tailer token is alive, not that it reads the session's real transcript: the agent's user starts the tailer (through sudo) and names its transcript, so it can stop the real one and start one of its own on a file it writes. A second tailer token for the run is a `tailer_redelegated` gap, but a decoy handed the first token (the agent's user can read the hook's memory) isn't. | **(1.5.3)** The transcript tailer checks the transcript lines it already read on every wake while that check is quick (under 10 ms, a transcript of about 25 MB); a larger one only once a hundred checks' time has passed, at most every 30 s, while the transcript keeps changing (a 400 MB one: about every 16 s; one of 800 MB or more: every 30 s). An edit to an already read line in that window is a `tailer_lost` gap only once the check runs, after the lines written meanwhile; one undone before then, or made just before the run ends while the transcript keeps changing, is none. | Finding an edit anywhere in what was read means reading all of it again: 0.3 s for 816 MB, which on every wake kept the tailer busy for much of a CPU core. | Keep coding-agent sessions short where in-place edits must be caught at once (`/clear` or a new session starts a new transcript); compare the run's transcript records with the transcript itself where it matters. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-the-signer-records-as-a-gap) |

A resumed session is read from where its transcript (Codex's rollout, Gemini CLI's chat recording) ended at SessionStart, so a harness that writes the earlier history only after that hook repeats it in the new run. **(1.5.1)** A Claude Code session that goes on after a SessionEnd with no SessionStart (a message to the session, a usage limit's reset) is read from where its transcripts ended at the new run's first hook: what Claude Code wrote between the SessionEnd and that hook (the new prompt; a first tool call's tool use, then a `reconcile.fabricated` gap) is read by no run, since the earlier run's tailer stopped at the SessionEnd. A call in that window whose hook didn't record it (a killed or timed-out hook) leaves no `reconcile.hook_missing` gap. | The hook can't see how far the earlier run's tailer got; it measures the transcript at its own calls, and a SessionEnd removes the session's state. | Read a session's runs in order (`tracekit view --dev`): each holds what was written while it was open. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |

A decide that differs from the model's tool use only there is no `reconcile.args_mismatch`, even when the hook, not Claude Code, made the difference; any other key or value is one, including a key a model makes up that isn't listed yet. Which runs are Claude Code's is the agent name the run registered, which the client gives: a run that says it is Claude Code gets the same leeway, for those keys only (no shipped rule reads them). An LLM gateway's record of a Claude Code call holds the arguments as the model sent them, before any of Claude Code's changes, so those changes (a Bash command's leading `cd`, Edit's `replace_all`, a key the tool doesn't take) read as `args_mismatch` there. | Claude Code hands its PreToolUse hook the tool use after its own checks and defaults, and its transcript keeps the model's keys; the list is the changes seen in Claude Code 2.1 transcripts against its hooks' input. | Read an `args_mismatch` on a Claude Code call against the call in its transcript: a key the model added that the tool doesn't take is Claude Code's own change. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| A Bash call's `description` is left out of that comparison from this release on, on both sides: the transcript tailer, which runs with the agent's Tracekit, and the signer. When the two run different releases (a remote or system signer upgraded apart from the agent's Tracekit), every Claude Code Bash call with a `description` can read as a false `reconcile.args_mismatch`. Noisy, never hidden: the mismatch is recorded, nothing is dropped. | Each side leaves out the keys its own release lists before it takes the digest it compares. | Upgrade the agent's Tracekit and the signer together; read an `args_mismatch` on a Bash call whose only difference is its `description` as this. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| **(1.5.1)** Claude Code's progress summary of a background agent can call SubagentHandback (its status line as the message) in a request no transcript records: its hook decides the call, which never completes ok, and the run gets a `reconcile.fabricated` for it, whose detail says what such a hand-back most likely is. | The tailer reads Claude Code's transcripts, and that request is in none of them, so the decide matches no tool use. | Read a `fabricated` SubagentHandback against the background agents' transcripts at that time: a short line of what one was doing while it went on working, with no hand-back reaching the parent, is Claude Code's summary. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#what-was-asked-and-said) |
| **(1.2)** Writes refused because their run is final are counted per run in memory and written about once a minute as a `late_activity` gap in the tenant's signer run; counts since the last write are lost if the signer stops uncleanly. A run-set lists them with the tenant-level gaps. | The writes can't go into a final run, and the signer-level refusal summary doesn't name the run. | Read `late_activity` in a run-set; if it recurs, check that each client registers a new run once its run ends. | [format v2](format-v2.md#3-events) |
| **(1.5.3)** A signer that starts after downtime closes, before it serves a call, every run whose last record is older than `idle_s` (an hour by default) and no approval of it still waits: the downtime counts as idle time. A client's next write for a closed run is refused `run_closed`, and it registers a new run (a coding-agent hook does so at once). | The idle clock runs on the records' wall-clock times, and no run can close while no signer runs. | Keep a signer's downtime under `idle_s`, or raise `idle_s`, where runs must span a restart. | [format v2](format-v2.md#3-events) |
| **(1.2)** Trailing client counter values become a gap only when the client says where its stream ended (`close_run` `last_client_seq`), and only for streams the closing identity wrote (or that never reached the signer); after a signer restart with no snapshot since, a stream written before it can't be ended that way. A gateway whose identity lacks the `close_run` grant logs the refusal on stderr and leaves no gap. | The signer can't know of values a client never sent, so the gap is the closing identity's word, and its reason names that identity; values up to there that still arrive in the grace period are recorded with no second gap. | Close runs with `last_client_seq` from your own clients; grant gateways `close_run` and stop them with SIGTERM; read a gap's reason for who reported it. | [gateway](gateway.md#what-is-recorded) |
