| A refusal Box made itself (a private or metadata address, too many connections: `tkbox.egress`) reaches the signer later with no time of its own, and one it lost is only counted (`tkbox.gap`), so TK-X212 places no call against either: each is `untimed_refusal` on the door side, and a fetch after one to that host is `box_refusal`. A door decision that let a connection through means Box allowed it, not that the host answered: an allowed connection whose dial then failed still matches a fetch, so `door_refused` is only for fetches every connection of which Box refused. | Box sends its own refusals through a rate-limited queue; its decisions come before the dial. | Read the box's events for those refusals and failed dials (`tkbox events`). | [findings](findings.md#boxes-what-tracekit-box-saw-tk-x212) |
| On a box run the per-call checks skip the box run's own calls (Box's doors), but TK-X204 checks the box's patch against the operator's task (`tkbox.task`: `keep_away`, `only_change` and `read_only`). Commands the box ran or connections it made are not checked against the task. | A box run's Write decisions are Box's, written back at apply, not the agent's edits: the per-call checks read them as an agent's and flagged every honest apply. | Review commands and network connections against the task yourself. | [findings](findings.md#boxes-what-tracekit-box-saw-tk-x212) |
| A file written back with `tkbox apply --dir` to a directory under the workspace looks like a file deeper in the workspace: when a path the agent wrote ends the patch's path, it is `apply_dir`, not checked; with `--dir` elsewhere every file is `apply_dir`. | Box's Write decision names the file on the host, not the apply directory. | Apply to the workspace the box was started with. | [findings](findings.md#boxes-what-tracekit-box-saw-tk-x212) |

## Viewer

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| A signed run gets its own graph only when the viewer has no why logs: with `--why DIR`, a `why/` folder in the signer's data dir, or `view.logs`, a run without a why log has no GRAPH. Each verified run is imported again whenever it grows, and its graph goes once its rows leave the live view (past `TRACEKIT_OBSERVE_MAX_RECORDS`). | The import fills the why workspace that why logs otherwise fill; re-importing a whole run keeps it current simply; a graph kept for every run the viewer ever showed grew without bound (some 3.4 KiB a record). | Open the store without `--why` to see every run's graph; with a `why/` folder in the data dir, import the run into a new folder and open that with `--why` (`tracekit why import --store DIR --run ID --out NEWDIR`; for a Postgres store, or a run that left the live view, `--bundle` its exported bundle). A file store's run views name the command for the mode the viewer runs in (a Postgres store's name `--store`, which needs the `--bundle` route instead). Expect a delay on very long runs. | [viewer](viewer.md#a-signed-runs-graph) |
| The viewer's verdicts are operator-side, not evidence. | The viewer runs on the operator's side. | Download the bundle and verify it with your own verifier and trust config. | [viewer](viewer.md#pages-and-api) |
| v2 records keep a call's arguments only as a salted commitment, so the viewer shows rule reasons, not arguments; for other policies, only rule ids. | Records never hold arguments. | Ask for `tracekit signer reveal` on the record when an audit needs it. | [viewer](viewer.md#laptop-viewer) |
| A run that fails verification shows only its failure report. | Records of a failed run aren't shown as if they were evidence. | Investigate with `tracekit verify`. | [viewer](viewer.md#pages-and-api) |
| A run stays `PENDING` until a checkpoint covers its last record. | The verdict needs a checkpoint. | Wait for the next checkpoint. | [viewer](viewer.md#laptop-viewer) |
| When the viewer's verification thread stops on an unexpected error, the page says verification stopped and no run is checked again until `tracekit view` restarts. While the store can't be read (a line the reader can't parse) or its checkpoint note is gone or older than one the viewer saw, no run is checked and the page says verification stopped too, until the store can be checked again. A run whose own check fails shows `NOT CHECKED` and is checked again 5 minutes later or when it grows. | One thread checks every run and nothing restarts it; the store is read whole, so one unreadable line stops every run's check; a signed note never goes back, so an older one means the store was edited or replaced; one run's error is kept to that run. | Restart `tracekit view`; repair or restore the store the message names; for a run you need now, export it and run `tracekit verify`. | [viewer](viewer.md#laptop-viewer) |
| The viewer's printed token is one operator credential over every tenant's runs. | It is the local operator's access. | Use OIDC roles for auditors and approvers. | [viewer](viewer.md#who-sees-what) |
| **(1.4)** The viewer's session cookie (the token's, or an OIDC session's) goes to every port of the viewer's host name: a page served by another program on that host, at another port (an agent's dev server on `127.0.0.1:3000`) that you open in the same browser receives it and can read the viewer as you until the cookie ends. The token's cookie ends after 8 hours or when the viewer stops; an OIDC session after 8 hours. | Browsers scope cookies by host name, not port, and the page needs a cookie: the token stays out of its URLs. | Open the viewer under a host name nothing else on the machine serves (`127.0.0.1` for it, `localhost` for the rest), or in a browser profile of its own; restart the viewer to end every token session at once. | [viewer](viewer.md#pages-and-api) |
| TIPS sees only what signed records and Claude Code's transcripts hold. It reads no other harness's transcripts, so a Codex or Cursor agent's steps count only when a gateway or SDK signed their usage. A signed record's usage holds no cache lifetime, so those steps are left out of the cache tip. A signed step is matched to the transcripts by a tool_use id or by identical token counts within 5 minutes of one transcript message: an unrelated message with the same counts that close is counted once too, and a signed message without tool calls whose counts differ from the transcript's (a gateway that signs other counts) is counted twice. The context replay counts tokens, not the work redone when a summary drops a detail, and its savings are estimates. Where a Claude Code hold ran (main session or subagent) and how long it blocked the agent come only from its transcript. Dollars are list prices for Anthropic's models, not a bill; the sessions of another model are left out, with a note naming it. Each time it's worked out (at most once a minute, or on READ AGAIN) it reads every transcript written in the last 7 days again: a 983 MB folder took 6.7 s and 370 MB of memory. | The transcript tailer records no token usage, the v2 usage field has no cache lifetime, the gateway signs exchanges under ids of its own, a Claude Code run's records decide a subagent's calls under the session's run, nothing records what a summary lost, and the reader keeps no offsets between reads. | Read THE ARITHMETIC before acting on a tip; start the viewer with `--transcripts DIR` (or `--dev`) so holds are placed and timed, and point it at one project's folder when the whole folder is large; update the price table in `tracekit/tips.py` when prices change. | [viewer](viewer.md#tips) |
| TIPS reads the signed records the viewer holds: its live view, the newest 50,000 rows (`TRACEKIT_OBSERVE_MAX_RECORDS`). On a busy store they cover less than the 7 days it reads from the transcripts, so held calls and signed token counts from before that are missing. | The viewer keeps its live view in memory; TIPS reads it rather than the store again. | Read the span on TIPS's first line; for a longer window raise `TRACEKIT_OBSERVE_MAX_RECORDS`, or use `tracekit report` for the store's holds over a week. | [viewer](viewer.md#tips) |
| The timeline is scaled to the calls shown, not the clock. | It shows sequence, not duration. | Read times from the records. | [viewer](viewer.md#laptop-viewer) |
| Why logs are shown to the printed token only; an OIDC session gets none of them. | A why log belongs to no tenant, so the viewer can't scope it. | Give investigators the token, or run `tracekit view --why DIR` for them on their own logs. | [viewer](viewer.md#why) |
| FIND THE CAUSE and RUN TEST run only for programs passed with `--allow-program`; otherwise the WHY panel shows the command to run. | A replay imports and runs the program a run names. | Start the viewer with `--allow-program module:SYSTEM`, or run the printed `tracekit why` command. | [viewer](viewer.md#why) |
| A signed call is joined to its why event only when that event cites the signed record it produced and both name the same tool; why logs without the citation (recorded without a signer, or imported from v1) show in GRAPH, AGENTS and INFLUENCE but not in a signed call's detail. | The citation is the only link between the two logs. | Record with the signer-backed why runtime, or open the logs alone with `tracekit view --why DIR`. | [viewer](viewer.md#why) |
| A joint cause FIND THE CAUSE reports is marked in the candidate list only until the page reloads; its summary line says it either way. | Joint groups aren't saved per candidate. | Re-run FIND THE CAUSE, or read the summary. | [viewer](viewer.md#why) |
| The why workspace reads every why log on each refresh. | It is recomputed rather than cached. | Keep the `--why` directory to the runs under investigation. | [viewer](viewer.md#why) |
| The viewer's why views have no links of their own (Causeway's `#/run/<id>/why/<call>`), and a candidate cause has no COPY COMMAND button. | The page is one live view of a server, not a set of routes; the command is printed instead. | Name the run in the picker; copy the printed `tracekit why test` command from the WHY panel. | [viewer](viewer.md#why-did-it-happen) |
| The static why report (`tracekit why report`, and the demo's `report.html`) runs no replays: **FIND THE CAUSE** and **RUN TEST** show the command to run, and it shows the runs as they were when it was written. | It is one file with the runs embedded, opened without a server. | Run the command it shows, or open the folder with `tracekit view --why DIR --allow-program SPEC`. | [why](why.md#demo) |
| One `tracekit view` shows one source: a v2 signer's store (or a central deployment's logs), a v1 ledger or bundle, or why logs alone; the nav links only the pages that source has. | Each source is its own feed, and the page shows one feed. | Start a second `tracekit view` on another `--port` for another source. | [viewer](viewer.md) |
| On a laptop no page answers a held call: the viewer's APPROVALS page lists each one with the commands that answer it, and the server's `/approvals` page, which answers, needs OIDC login and `view.signer`. | The page answers for a signed-in person over the signer RPC; the printed token is no person. | Answer with `tracekit approvals`, or configure [viewer login](identity.md#viewer-login). | [approvals](approvals.md) |
| `tracekit view --ledger` and `--bundle` serve loopback only, and have no `--prices` or `--export`. | They keep `tracekit observe`'s rules for the v1 ledger. | Reach the page through an SSH tunnel; for cost or a replay file run `tracekit observe --prices FILE` or `tracekit observe --export FILE`. | [viewer](viewer.md) |
| **(1.5.1)** `--data-dir DIR` (of `tracekit view`, `report`, `export --v2`, `signer trust` and `signer vkey`) reads a data dir without its signer.yaml: `signer trust --data-dir` pins the log key only, not the config's witnesses or Rekor anchor, and `export --v2 --data-dir` can't ask the signer for a checkpoint, so a run none covers yet isn't exported. Both say so when it matters. A data dir of a Postgres-backed signer holds no store. | A data dir names no socket and no witnesses; only the config does. | Pass the signer's `--config` to pin its witnesses or to have export ask for a checkpoint; or export again after the signer's next checkpoint (within seconds). | [viewer](viewer.md#laptop-viewer) |

## Weekly report (`tracekit report`)

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| It reports on one store at a time. Each laptop's dev signer has its own store, so a team's laptops are reported one by one, and nothing adds them up. | It reads a signer's store directly, as the viewer does; it collects nothing from other machines. | Run it on each laptop and send the pages, or point the agents at one signer (`tracekit init --v2 --remote URL`) and report on its store with `--config`. | [viewer](viewer.md#weekly-report) |
| It is an operator summary, not evidence: nothing in it is signed, and its verdicts pin the log key the store names (`dev` assurance on a laptop). | It runs on the operator's side, over the operator's own store. | Export the runs that matter (`tracekit export --v2 --run ID`) and have the auditor verify them with keys they pinned. | [auditor guide](auditor-guide.md) |
| It checks every run in the window with the verifier before it prints, as the viewer does when it starts: a week of 48 Claude Code runs (245,412 records, 63,000 tool calls) took about 3.5 minutes on a laptop. Most of that is the verifier's schema and signature checks, and each run's export scanning the whole log for key records. | Its counts come only from records that verified. | Use a shorter `--since`, or `--tenant`; it says how many runs and records it is checking before it starts. | [viewer](viewer.md#weekly-report) |
| An example call shows its arguments only with `--dev` while the dev signer runs, each read a signed `content.accessed` record. With `--config` or `--data-dir`, with no dev signer running, or for arguments redaction changed (a secret), it reads `arguments not shown (the record holds only their salted hash)`. | Records hold arguments only as a salted commitment; only the dev signer keeps a copy this user may read, and redacted arguments no longer open their commitment. | Start the dev signer (`tracekit up`) before the report; for one record, `tracekit signer reveal`. | [content vault](privacy.md#the-content-vault) |
| A rule's description comes from the policy the signer ran: a shipped pack, the config's `policy` file, or the copy the signer keeps in its data dir's `policies/` (readable by the signer's user only). Run as another user, a rule of a policy it can't read shows its id only, and isn't counted as why a call was denied or flagged: the report lists it as not placed and says how many calls cite such a policy. | A rule id means what the policy whose hash the decision records says, so no other policy's text or section is used for it. | Run the report as the signer's user, or pass the signer's `--config` with its `policy` file. | [viewer](viewer.md#weekly-report) |
| An engine check (`TK-SHELL-PARSE`, `TK-UNKNOWN-TOOL`, `TK-QUARANTINE`, ...) that matched a denied or flagged call is listed as not placed, never counted as why: it may be why (an unknown tool flagged, a quarantined run's call denied) or not (`TK-SHELL-PARSE` asks by default, and a deny rule denied the call). | A decision records every rule that matched, not which one decided it, and an engine check has no section of the policy; its decision is a policy setting (`on_parse_error`, `unknown_tools`). | Open the call in `tracekit view`, which shows every rule that matched it. | [viewer](viewer.md#weekly-report) |
| Before it places or counts a run, it checks every record of the store against the newest checkpoint, in every tenant (`--tenant` too) and the signer's own runs: its hash is its event's, it is filed under the run its event names, and the hashes make the checkpoint's root (3.6 seconds for a store of 246,344 records and 845 runs on a laptop, whatever `--since` or `--tenant`). A run holding an edited record is listed `FAILED` whatever its tenant and times, a signer's run or another tenant's included; when the hashes don't make the root (a hash was rewritten too), the store and every run fail; and the command exits 1. Records newer than the checkpoint are not checked against it and place no run: a run whose covered records all fall before the window isn't listed, even when newer records of it fall in the window. The report says how many records of the store are newer, and a run no checkpoint covers yet is `PENDING`. | The store files a record under the tenant, run id and time the record holds, and only the signed checkpoint vouches for them; in dev mode the agent's user can edit the store. | Run it again after the next checkpoint (the signer writes one within seconds); for a `FAILED` run, `tracekit view` shows its first failing check. | [viewer](viewer.md#weekly-report) |
| `--share` prints an agent name only when Tracekit's own hooks and tools give it (`claude-code`, `codex`, `agentsh`, ...), and a rule id only when it is a shipped pack's, an engine check's or the signer's: every other agent counts as `other`, every rule of a policy of your own as `custom rule` (in `tracekit audit --share` too). A report from SDK agents or a policy of your own says little about them. | A run's name is any string its code chose (a script's file name for `tracekit.instrument()`, a customer, a project), an agent can register its run under any name, and a policy's author chooses its ids: Tracekit can't tell a private name from a public one. | Say in the report, in your own words, what your `other` agents and `custom rule` rules are; read the block before you post it (`tracekit report --share` says what it counts as `other` on stderr). | [field reports](field-reports.md#what-is-shared-and-what-isnt) |
| It reads the v2 signer's store only, not the v1 ledger (`tracekitd`), why logs or bundles. It summarises no signer's run (its key records, content reads, refused writes, a witness that didn't countersign): it checks their records and lists one only when it fails. | It summarises the agents' runs of the store `tracekit view` verifies. | `tracekit view` shows the signer's own rows; `tracekit view --ledger` the v1 ledger. | [viewer](viewer.md#laptop-viewer) |
| `tracekit feedback` (and its `--file`) fills in the week's counts from the dev signer's store only: the store of a signer started from a signer.yaml, another data dir or a remote signer isn't read, and with no dev store the link or file says so instead. | It takes `report --share`'s week from the store a laptop's dev signer keeps; it has no `--config` or `--data-dir`. | Run `tracekit report --share --since 7d --config signer.yaml` (or `--data-dir DIR`) and paste its block into the report. | [field reports](field-reports.md#file-a-report) |

## Audit (`tracekit audit`)

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| It has readers for Claude Code's and Cursor's transcripts, Cline's tasks and Gemini CLI's sessions only, and sees only the tool calls they hold: nothing the agent did outside a tool call. Another agent's sessions (Codex, say) are read only once turned into a calls file. A Cursor transcript gives no time per call, so `--since` goes by each file's modification time (a file written in the window counts whole), and no call id, so a copied call counts again. Cline's tasks from before 4.0 are read from VS Code's own global storage only (`Code`), not from Insiders', VSCodium's or another editor's: give such a folder as ROOT with `--agent cline`. | Each harness writes its transcripts in its own format, and each needs a reader of its own. | Turn the agent's sessions or log into a [calls file](audit.md#calls-file) and run `tracekit audit --calls FILE --agent NAME`; for what an agent does from now on, record it with Tracekit (`tracekit init --dev --v2`). | [audit](audit.md) |
| Gemini CLI's sessions older than 30 days aren't there to read, and those its macOS Seatbelt sandbox keeps aren't read by default. | Gemini CLI deletes sessions older than 30 days by default (`general.sessionRetention`), and under its Seatbelt sandbox (`SANDBOX=sandbox-exec`) keeps its runtime state, sessions included, in `~/.cache/.gemini/tmp` (under `$GEMINI_CLI_HOME` when set), not `~/.gemini/tmp`. | Set a longer `general.sessionRetention.maxAge` in Gemini CLI's settings to keep sessions for audit (deleted ones can't be recovered); for sandboxed sessions, run `tracekit audit ~/.cache/.gemini/tmp --agent gemini`. | [audit](audit.md#what-it-reads) |
| Its counts are evidence of nothing: the agent could have edited the transcript, and each call is decided by today's policy, not the one in force when it ran. | Tracekit never recorded these calls; only a signed record shows what happened. | Use it to see what Tracekit would hold; for evidence, record runs with the signer and export bundles. | [audit](audit.md) |
| It can count fewer holds than the signer would: each call is decided on its own, without the run's state, so `from: untrusted` rules never match (there is no provenance), and detect sequences, limits and the person's prompt constraints don't apply. | It reads a transcript, not a run; that state exists only in the signer. | Treat its counts as a floor; the signer's own decisions are the ones that hold. | [audit](audit.md) |
| It keeps every call it reads in memory (about 1 KB each, 2 KB with `--json`; `--show` text is cut to 72 characters), so a very large transcript folder takes memory in proportion. | The JSON report and `--all` list every call, so it keeps them all until the counts are done. | Use `--since` to replay a shorter window. | [audit](audit.md) |

## Model receipts (`tracekit verify-model`)

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| A weights file's receipt is the hook's report (`reported_by: hook`), not something the signer saw: the agent's own user runs the hook, so the agent can change the file after the hook hashed it, or report other hashes through its run token. | The hook is the agent side's process; nothing outside the agent reads the file yet. | Treat a receipt as the agent side's claim. A receipt computed outside the agent (the tool executor, a signer-side sensor) is planned. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| Only the coding-agent hooks take receipts, and only for Write, Edit, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit and Bash calls: an MCP server's write tool, or a framework integration's call, gets none. `verify-model` counts a receipt only in the result of one of those five tools whose arguments, read back from the vault, name a file the hook hashes; the hook sets `model_receipts` on those tools' results only, and removes one it didn't set. Any other receipt is ignored (`receipt(s) ignored`), and every other tool's result is recorded as it came, so a receipt in an MCP server's or a web page's output never counts. That stops a tool's output, not the agent: a matching receipt is `recorded by the agent-side hook`, which runs as the agent's user, and a client of the agent's own that speaks the RPC itself sends what it likes. | Only the hook hashes the file, on the agent's side, and the record keeps every other tool's result as the tool returned it, for provenance. Arguments redaction changed open no commitment, so they name no file. | Read a matching receipt as the agent side's record of the file, not a third party's. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| A receipt is taken only for a call that succeeded (a failed, interrupted or `is_error` call gets none), after the call ended: a parallel call, or another process, that writes the same file before the hook reads it puts its write into the receipt. | The hook hashes the file when the call's PostToolUse hook runs, and a harness can run calls in parallel. | Read `hashed after tool call` as what the file held then; write a weights file from one call at a time. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| `tracekit verify-model` needs the signer's kept content and the `content_read` grant: the dev signer keeps a run's content for 7 days, and a configured signer keeps none unless `content.retain` is on. A bundle alone can't be checked offline. | The record holds only a salted commitment of the call's result; the receipt is in the result, which only the vault keeps. | Check within the retention window, or set `content: {retain: true}` and grant `content_read`. A check from the bundle alone needs receipts in the record, a format change planned for when a lab asks for it. | [content vault](privacy.md#the-content-vault) |
| A call gets a receipt only for a path the policy's parser reads it writing: a write tool's path, a shell redirection, a `cp`, `mv` or `install` destination, and the like. A script that writes weights itself (`python edit.py`), a shell download (`curl -o`, `huggingface-cli download`) or an archive extraction gets none (Crush's `download` tool is decided as a Write, so it gets a receipt of the file on disk when the hook reads it), and a path the hook would have to guess is refused: one starting with `~`; every path of a command line in which `cp`, `mv`, `install` or `ln` has `-t` (in a cluster too, `-vt`), `git`, `env`, `make` or `tar` has `-C`, `sudo` has `-D`, or one of them has `--target-directory`, `--directory` or `--chdir` or a prefix of one that GNU accepts (`env --ch=sub`); and a relative one in a command that changes directory (`cd`, `pushd`, `popd`, `git --work-tree`, or a shell call's own working directory: Gemini CLI's and Qwen Code's `dir_path` or `directory`, Cursor's `working_directory`, Codex's `workdir`, agy's `Cwd` (in any case), Crush's `working_dir`, whatever its value). Another command's `-t` or `-C` (`sort -t,`, `grep -C3`) refuses nothing, and another command's directory option isn't read: a relative path there is hashed in the working directory. | The hook reads the call's arguments; it doesn't watch the file system, expand `~` or follow every command's directory options. | Have the script write a staging file and move it into place with `mv` in a call of its own: the move gets a receipt. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| A write that lands after the hook reads gets no receipt: every weights path is refused for a Bash call with `run_in_background` (Qwen Code's `is_background`), input typed into another running session (Codex's `write_stdin`, agy's `manage_task` `send_input`, Devin's `write_to_process`), Qwen Code's `monitor`, Kilo's `background_process`, a command line that runs `systemd-run`, `at`, `batch` or `setsid -f` (`--fork`; plain `setsid` runs in the foreground), and one whose text holds a lone `&` (`cp new.pt m.pt &`; not `&&`, `2>&1`, `&>` or `\|&`) or `coproc`. A `&` inside quotes (`curl 'https://x/?a=1&b=2' -o m.pt`) refuses the line too. Codex's `exec_command` sends no post-tool hook while its command still runs when the call returns (after `yield_time_ms`), so that call gets no receipt and is completed as an error when the session ends. `screen -dm`, `tmux new -d`, `start-stop-daemon -b` and `daemonize` name no path the policy's parser reads, so their calls get none. | The hook reads the file when the call's post-tool hook runs, which for these is when the command starts or the input is sent, not when the write ends. The policy's shell parser keeps no mark of a `&`, so the hook reads it off the command's raw text, quotes and all. | Write weights in a call that waits for the write to end (no `&`, not in the background), then move them into place with `cp` or `mv` in a call of its own. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| Every path of a command line that runs `ssh`, `chroot`, `nsenter`, `unshare` or `firejail` is refused, and a remote `scp` or `rsync` target (`host:/m.safetensors`) is refused as a file the hook can't find. A runner whose command the parser doesn't read into paths (`docker exec`, `kubectl exec`) names no path, so its call gets no receipt. | Such a command writes on another host, under another root, or in another mount namespace or sandbox; the hook reads only the file it sees on this host. | Hash the file where it was written, or copy it back to this host with `cp` or `mv` in a call of its own: that write gets a receipt. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| A file past 8 GiB, a `.safetensors` header past 448 KiB (more tensors than a receipt takes, or more than 256 KiB of metadata; refused before it is read), tensor hashes past 96 KiB (about 800 tensors), or a file its format doesn't allow (offsets outside the file, a gap between tensors) gets a reason in place of a receipt (`refused`). A call's result holds at most 4 receipts, then a count of the rest; receipts that would take the result past half a frame (a large result kept whole with `content_capture: full`) are replaced by a marker that says so, or dropped without one when the marker doesn't fit either. | The call's result must fit one 1 MiB frame and the vault's 1 MiB per record. | Shard large models (the Hugging Face Hub shards at 5 GB by default). | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| The hook hashes for at most 10 s per call, then refuses the receipts it hadn't finished (`out of time`); one read that blocks (a hung network file system) can still outlast it. Until then hashing delays the agent's next step: about 0.7 s per GiB of `.safetensors` on a laptop, measured in memory (longer from disk). | The same PostToolUse hook, with a 30 s timeout, sends the call's result: hashing must not cost it. | Keep the weights an agent writes on a local disk. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| Only `.safetensors` gets a hash per tensor; `.gguf`, `.pt`, `.pth` and `.ckpt` get the file's hash only, so `verify-model` says only whether the file differs. Other weights files (`pytorch_model.bin`, `.onnx`, `.h5`) get no receipt. A tensor's dtype and shape aren't checked against its bytes: a file the safetensors library won't load for that still gets a receipt. | It reads one format's layout. | Save weights as `.safetensors` to see which tensors changed. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md#weights-files-a-call-wrote) |
| A Bash, Write or Edit call the policy marks `untrusted` gets no receipts, and the hook removes a `model_receipts` key from its result. | Its result goes to the signer as the tool returned it, for provenance, and a gateway's verified tool results compare it as sent to the model; a receipts key in it is never the hook's. | Keep shell and write tools out of `untrusted`. The shipped packs list `http`, `mcp`, `WebFetch` and `WebSearch` (coding), `http`, `browser` and `mcp` (server), `browser` (browser) and `http` (server-net): none of them a tool the hook receipts. | [policy](policy-v2.md#provenance-untrusted-and-from-untrusted) |
| A receipt reaches the signer in clear, inside the result: its vault keeps the file's path, size, hashes and tensor names even with `content_capture: hashed`. | `verify-model` reads the receipt back from the vault. | Keep secrets out of weights file paths; erase a run's content when you no longer need it. | [content vault](privacy.md#the-content-vault) |

## Install

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| On an Intel Mac or a musl Linux (Alpine), `npm install @cygnuxlabs/tracekit` gets no platform signer package, so the dev signer needs Python. | Signer packages are built for four platforms. Intel Macs lack usable `google-re2` and `cryptography` wheels, and none is built for musl. | `pip install tracekit-ai`, or point the client at a running signer. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md#install) |
| `pip install tracekit-ai` also installs `cryptography`, `rfc8785`, `google-re2` (Python 3.10+) and `regex`. | The dev signer starts from that one install. It signs, canonicalises JSON and runs its policy engine. | To get the verifier alone, `pip install --no-deps tracekit-ai`. It runs on the standard library. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#1-install) |
| On a Python that refuses `pip install` outside a venv (PEP 668: `error: externally-managed-environment`; Debian 12, Ubuntu 23.04 and later, Homebrew's Python), `pip install tracekit-ai` fails. `pipx install tracekit-ai` and `uv tool install tracekit-ai` give only the `tracekit` command: an SDK agent can't import Tracekit from them. | These Pythons leave their own packages to the system's package manager. pipx and `uv tool` put each command in a venv of its own, which no other program imports from. | For an SDK agent, `pip install tracekit-ai` in the agent's own venv. For the command alone (`tracekit last`, `tracekit view`, `tracekit approvals`, Claude Code's hooks through `tracekit init --dev --v2`), pipx or `uv tool`. With both, keep them at the same version: they share the dev signer. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#1-install) |
| Upgrading Tracekit doesn't upgrade the Python your hooks run, nor the dev signer already running. `tracekit doctor` (`D-VERSIONS`) asks the Python of each hook init wrote which Tracekit it imports, and reads the running dev signer's version. It can't name the Python the Claude Code plugin's hook runs, nor that of a hook command init didn't write (it says so). It runs only the commands of your own hook files, with absolute paths: a project's hook files (in the directory doctor runs in), relative paths, and a file that a relative `HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `CRUSH_GLOBAL_CONFIG` or `TRACEKIT_CLIENT_HOME` places are reported, not probed. As root it runs no hook's Python. In system mode it isn't run: `D-HOOKS-VENV` checks that the hooks run `/opt/tracekit`'s Python, the one doctor runs from, but a system signer still running an older version after `/opt/tracekit` was upgraded in place isn't compared. A pre-release or dev build compares as its release. | The plugin picks its Python as it runs (the first of `TRACEKIT_PYTHON`, `python3` and `python` on Claude Code's `PATH` that imports Tracekit), in Claude Code's environment, not doctor's. Doctor never runs a project's commands: running it in a cloned repository runs none of its code. A relative path resolves against doctor's directory, not the hook's. Doctor won't run, as root, a Python that a file the agent's user can write names. | Upgrade as [upgrading](quickstart-v2.md#upgrading) says and run `tracekit doctor`. With the plugin, read its hook's notice (next row) or ask the Python it finds: `python3 -I -c 'import tracekit; print(tracekit.__version__)'`. In system mode, stop the signer, upgrade and start it again ([upgrading](deploy.md#upgrading)). | [doctor](doctor.md#v2-checks) |
| A hook whose signer runs another Tracekit version says so once per session, with the fix, on the first event it lets through, never beside a block: in Claude Code as a `systemMessage`, for the other coding agents on stderr, which some of them don't show for a call they allow. The session's state keeps that it was said; a session whose state ended or was pruned (30 days) hears it again. An agent can stop the notice by editing that state (in dev mode it runs as you): it is a notice, not a control. | A hook's output reaches the person only where its harness shows it, and a block's message is what the agent reads: the notice stays out of it. The session state lives in the agent's own runtime dir. | Run `tracekit doctor` after each upgrade: `D-VERSIONS` checks every hook and the signer. | [upgrading](quickstart-v2.md#upgrading) |
| A `--no-deps` install verifies bundles but can't run a signer. `tracekit up` and `tracekit init --dev --v2` refuse to start. | The signer needs the policy engine and `cryptography`. Neither is in the standard library. | `pip install tracekit-ai` | [doctor](doctor.md) |
| Where `google-re2` has no wheel (a new Python, an uncommon platform), pip builds it from source, and without a C++ toolchain and abseil the whole install fails. | pip has no fallback between dependencies. The signer itself uses `regex` when `google-re2` isn't importable. | `pip install --no-deps tracekit-ai`, then `pip install cryptography "rfc8785>=0.1,<0.2" regex` (with their own dependencies) | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#1-install) |
| **(1.2)** Claude Code's hooks go to `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/settings.json` when it is set, else `~/.claude/settings.json`. `tracekit uninstall` and `tracekit doctor` look at both, and at the one file `tracekit init` last recorded; hooks an earlier init put under another `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` aren't found from a shell that doesn't set it. | Only the shell that installed them knew the directory, and init records one settings file, the last it put hooks in. | Uninstall with the same `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` you installed with. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#1-install) |
| **(1.3)** The Claude Code plugin runs the v2 hooks. Updating it from a v1 plugin moves v1 dev mode to the v2 dev signer: the v1 policy and `tracekit pending` no longer apply, and the v1 ledger can be bridged into the v2 log only until the v2 signer records something. In v1 system mode every tool call is blocked until v2 system mode is set up. | The plugin carries one hook, and the v2 hook needs a v2 signer. | Before the next session: export the v1 runs you need, `tracekit uninstall`, `tracekit signer bridge`, `tracekit init --dev --v2 --no-hooks`; system mode: `tracekit init --v2 --user <agent-user> --no-hooks` as root, by one of [deploy](deploy.md#laptop-system-mode)'s routes: from /opt's venv (`sudo /opt/tracekit/bin/python -I -m tracekit init …`), with Tracekit pip-installed into the system Python (`sudo /usr/bin/python3 -I -m tracekit init …`), or from a root-owned clone (`sudo /usr/bin/python3 -m tracekit init …`, run in the clone). | [plugin](../plugin/README.md#upgrading-from-the-v1-plugin) |
| **(1.3)** With the Claude Code plugin and `tracekit init`'s hooks both in place, every call is decided twice and every approved call is blocked. `tracekit init --dev --v2` recognises Tracekit's plugin across marketplaces when Claude Code has an install record for it and the current marketplace catalog names `Cygnux-Labs/Tracekit` at `plugin/`; system init doesn't check. It can still miss a plugin enabled only through `--plugin-dir`, `--settings` or `defaultEnabled`, one from a claude.ai-synced marketplace (which has no `known_marketplaces.json` entry), or one whose install record or catalog is unavailable. The catalog is current metadata and may no longer describe cached files from an older install. | Both hooks decide the call, and one consumes the approval the other waits on. | Keep one: `tracekit uninstall` or use the plugin key shown by `tracekit init` or `tracekit doctor` in `/plugin disable <key>`. `tracekit doctor` warns when both run (`D-HOOKS-PRESENT`). With the plugin, run system init with `--no-hooks`. | [plugin](../plugin/README.md#the-plugin-or-tracekit-init-not-both) |
| **(1.3)** The Claude Code plugin without the `tracekit` package lets every event run unrecorded, with a notice in the transcript, until Tracekit is set up for the user (the dev signer's data dir exists, or `TRACEKIT_PYTHON` or `TRACEKIT_SIGNER` is set); from then on it blocks tool calls. | Blocking someone who installed the plugin but not Tracekit would lock them out of Claude Code with nothing yet to protect. | `pip install tracekit-ai==<the plugin's version> && tracekit init --dev --v2 --no-hooks` (the plugin's messages name the version) | [plugin](../plugin/README.md#when-a-hook-cant-run) |
| Hooks `tracekit init --dev` installs from a source checkout (an editable `pip install -e` or the source tree itself) import Tracekit from that working tree each time they run. A branch switch, a rebase or other code checked out there changes what every session's hooks run. Code there that doesn't import fails every hooked event: the v2 hooks block each tool call until it imports again. When init ran from a git working tree with git on init's PATH, and git is on the hook's PATH, the hook then prints one line naming the working tree, the error, the branch it is on now and the branch (or commit) it was on at install, and how to fix it; otherwise it prints the traceback. | The dev hooks run the code you're working on, so a change takes effect without running init again. Nothing copies it. | Keep the checkout your hooks run from on a working branch, and switch branches in another worktree (`git worktree add`). Or install Tracekit with `pip install` (not `pip install -e`) and run `tracekit init --dev --v2` again (with `--agent NAME` for another coding agent), so the hooks run the installed package. After a switch, `git -C <checkout> checkout <branch>` puts the hooks back. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#1-install) |
| `sudo /opt/tracekit/bin/python -I -m tracekit uninstall --v2` changes nothing while the agent's Claude Code settings file is a symlink or not valid JSON, and says so. | Root edits that file as the agent's user, never through a link, and never overwrites what it can't parse. | Fix or remove the file as the agent's user, then uninstall again. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#system-mode-linux-macos-experimental) |
| `tracekit uninstall` gives a coding agent's settings file back its meaning, not its exact bytes. The file is rewritten with 2-space indents and a final newline. An empty `"hooks": {}` (and Claude Code's `"env": {}`) is dropped. A file init created stays as `{}` (Cursor's as `{"version": 1}`), Cursor's `"version": 1` stays in a file that had none, and the folders init made for OpenCode, goose and Droid stay. Each init or uninstall that changes a file first saves a copy beside it (`<file>.bak-<time>-<random>`), and nothing removes those. | Tracekit edits these files as JSON, not as text, and doesn't keep what a file looked like before init. The copies let you get any earlier version back. | To get a file back exactly, copy the `.bak-` copy your first init made over it, or delete the file if init created it. Delete the `.bak-` copies, and any empty file or folder init left, once you no longer need them. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| `tracekit uninstall --agent droid` deletes the `.factory/hooks.json` that init created only when what is left in it equals the hooks in `settings.json` beside it. If you changed the hooks in either file after init, or `settings.json` is gone or can't be read, `hooks.json` stays with Tracekit's hooks taken out, and Droid goes on running its hooks and ignoring `settings.json`'s. | Droid reads `settings.json`'s hooks only while `hooks.json` is absent, so init copies them into the `hooks.json` it creates; Tracekit doesn't keep a record of what it copied, so a changed file can't be told from one you made yourself. | Uninstall names the file it kept: move any hooks you want from it into `settings.json`, then delete it. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md) |
| `tracekit init --remote` (v1) after `tracekit init --dev` leaves the dev tracekitd running, and `tracekit uninstall` then doesn't stop it. | The remote client config replaces the dev one, which named the daemon's home. | Run `tracekit uninstall` before switching, or stop the pid in `~/.tracekit-signer/tracekitd.pid`. | [remote ingest](remote-ingest.md) |
| A `--no-deps` verifier is slower. | It falls back to pure-Python Ed25519 and JCS. Both are checked against `cryptography` and `rfc8785` and give the same verdict. | Install `cryptography` and `rfc8785` if you verify many large bundles. | [signing](signing.md) |

## One-line setup (`tracekit.instrument()`)

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| **(1.2)** A client keeps each run's event counter until the run is closed (`run.close()`) or the signer answers that it is: a server that lets conversations end by the signer's idle timeout keeps about 100–300 bytes per conversation (TypeScript: for the client's life; Python: at most 65,536 runs, the least recently used forgotten first, so a run idle while that many others were used restarts its count and its next events read as a gap). | A counter can't be dropped while the run may still take events: a reused run id would restart at 0. | Close each conversation's run when it ends, or give a batch of conversations a client of its own and close it. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#a-server-a-run-per-conversation) |
| **(1.2)** `instrument()`'s process client remembers which run each call, approval, exchange and stream went to, up to 65,536 of each, the oldest forgotten first: a call that waits for a person while that many newer ones are decided completes in the current run, which may refuse it. | Routing needs the run a call was decided in; the maps are bounded so a long-running process doesn't grow. | Use a run per conversation (`conversation.current()`); answer approvals before tens of thousands of newer calls. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#a-server-a-run-per-conversation) |
| **(1.2)** TypeScript: a run's event calls go out one at a time on one connection, and a call's timeout includes its wait for its turn, so a busy process run (`instrument()`'s, shared by every conversation) can time out on a signer that is only slow; the run's fail mode then applies. | Events reach the signer in client_seq order. | Give each conversation its own run (`withRun`), or raise `timeoutMs`. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** The Vercel AI SDK adapter keeps the arguments string of up to 4,096 tool calls whose tool has no `execute` when `toolApproval` isn't used. | It keeps the model's own arguments for the call's decide until the call runs. | Pass `toolApproval`, or wrap only tools with an `execute`. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** The Claude Agent SDK hooks (Python and TypeScript) leave a tool to the executor only by its exact name, `mcp__tracekit-executor__<action>` for an action of the app's own in-process executor server (Python: the one `executor_tools.claude_agent_sdk` built; TypeScript: the `executorActions` the app names), and only when the `mcp_servers` the app hands them hold that server under `tracekit-executor`. So they rely on the CLI connecting that server in place of a project `.mcp.json` server of the same key, as Claude Code 2.1.294 does. Every other tool is gated, a project server's whose name starts with `tracekit-executor` included. | The hooks see a tool's name, not which server the CLI routes the call to. | Set `strict_mcp_config=True` (the CLI's `--strict-mcp-config`), so the CLI loads only your app's MCP servers. | [executor](executor.md) |
| It only gates agents, tools, graphs, options and sessions built after it runs. | It wires each framework where its objects are built: `Agent`, `ToolNode`, `ClaudeAgentOptions`, `ClientSession.call_tool`, Browser Use's `Tools`, Pydantic AI's `Agent` (CrewAI's hooks are global: they gate every crew). | Make `import tracekit; tracekit.instrument()` the first line of your agent. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#2-one-line-in-your-agent) |
| It raises (in TypeScript, rejects with) `SignerUnavailable` when no signer answers, so the app can't start. | A run's fail mode is closed until a signer says otherwise. Without a run, no call could be recorded. | Follow the message. With no `TRACEKIT_SIGNER` set, it starts a dev signer itself. With it set, start that signer or unset it. In system mode, run `sudo /opt/tracekit/bin/python -I -m tracekit doctor`. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#first-run-errors) |
| A process has one run unless you open a run per conversation, and the run closes when the process exits. A killed process leaves its run open until the signer's idle timeout. A process run the signer closed after it was idle is replaced by a new one on the next call (a logged warning), linked to it as its `parent_run` only while the old run is still closing. | The run belongs to the process, not to any one agent loop or session. | `tracekit last` names a run that is still open and skips it. A server opens a run per conversation and makes it current per request: `with conversation.current():`. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#a-server-a-run-per-conversation) |
| **(1.2)** With `instrument()`, the current run (a run per conversation) reaches the threads and tasks that copy the context: asyncio tasks and `asyncio.to_thread` do; a plain `ThreadPoolExecutor.submit` or `threading.Thread` doesn't, so its calls go to the process run. | A `contextvars.ContextVar` follows the context it was set in. | Submit work with `contextvars.copy_context().run`, or make the run current inside the thread (`with conversation.current():`). Don't hold a `current()` block across a sync generator's `yield` that a server iterates in a thread pool (each step runs in a fresh context): make the run current inside each step, or use an async generator. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#a-server-a-run-per-conversation) |
| **(1.2)** A chat app that opens a new run per request while it resends the conversation's history gets a `reconcile.result_without_call` for each earlier tool result: the new run never saw those calls. | Results are recorded as sent in every request; the signer can only check them against the calls of the same run. | Keep one run per conversation, open across its requests (`conversation.current()`). | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#a-server-a-run-per-conversation) |
| An `ask` (a call waiting for a person) only completes where the framework can wait. OpenAI Agents interrupts the run, and your code must apply the decision. LangGraph pauses only with a checkpointer and denies the call without one. MCP waits up to 300 s in `call_tool`, Browser Use up to 150 s before the action. The Claude Agent SDK waits up to 9 minutes in its hook. | Waiting for a person is part of each framework's own control flow. | For approvals, wire the adapter by hand as its quickstart shows. | [OpenAI Agents](quickstarts/openai-agents.md), [LangGraph](quickstarts/langchain.md) |
| A framework version outside the tested range gets a warning. If its adapter can't be wired at all, it is skipped. | Each adapter hooks the framework's internals at one tested minor version. | Install the versions the warning names, or wire the adapter by hand. | [adapters](adapters.md) |
| Don't wire an adapter by hand in a process that also calls `instrument()`. | The signer would decide the same call twice. | Use one or the other. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#2-one-line-in-your-agent) |
| OpenAI Agents SDK hosted tools, computer use and handoffs are not gated. | They run outside the tool hooks. | Their calls are still in the signed model responses. | [OpenAI Agents](quickstarts/openai-agents.md) |
| Every MCP `ClientSession` in the process shares one signer stream, so their calls are recorded in one order. | A run allows 64 streams (`streams_per_run`), and a server agent may open a session per request. | For one stream per session, wrap each session with `TracekitSession` by hand. | [MCP](quickstarts/mcp.md) |
| A call decided without the model's tool-call id (CrewAI's hooks, an MCP session) is paired with the model's tool use only by arguments: same model turn, same tool name and arguments, and no other call of that turn alike (`paired_by_args` on `run.final`). Two identical calls in one turn, another agent's model call in the same run between the model's answer and the hook (CrewAI async tasks, or several agents in `instrument()`'s one process run), or a tool the adapter names differently from the model (mcp-agent's `<server>_<tool>` against `mcp:<server>/<tool>`), get `reconcile.hook_missing` and `reconcile.fabricated`. An `ask` in CrewAI waits at most 5 minutes in the hook. | The framework hands the hook no call id; pairing anything less certain could hide a call that ran ungated. One approval wait is capped at the signer's 300 s. | Read those runs' `reconcile.*` records as unpaired, not as ungated, when the model's tool names differ from the decided ones; give each crew or task its own run where agents run at once; answer CrewAI approvals within 5 minutes or the call is blocked and the model told. | [CrewAI](integrations/crewai.md#not-covered), [gateway](gateway.md#reconciliation) |
| **(1.2)** On CrewAI's text (ReAct) tool path, for models without native tool calling, CrewAI retries a failing tool inside one call of its hooks: the retries run under that call's one decision, and only the last outcome is recorded. | CrewAI's hooks run once around its tool use, not per attempt; nothing public is called between attempts. | Use a model with native tool calling, where each call is decided; or keep tools whose retries matter idempotent. | [CrewAI](integrations/crewai.md#not-covered) |
| Pydantic AI's text output functions (`TextOutput`), and argument rewrites in another capability's `before_tool_validate`, are not decided. A deferred tool (`ApprovalRequired`, `CallDeferred`) resumed under the same call id is decided again as its next attempt, and its first attempt reads as `decided_not_completed`. | The `Tracekit` capability decides tool calls and output tool calls; a text output function has no tool call, and validation runs before the arguments it compares. | Register a function with side effects as a tool or a `ToolOutput`; rewrite arguments in `before_tool_execute` or `wrap_tool_execute`, where the change is recorded as an error. | [Pydantic AI](integrations/pydantic-ai.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Pydantic AI and Microsoft Agent Framework: a call the framework rejects before Tracekit sees it (Pydantic AI: arguments that fail validation, a tool the agent doesn't have; Agent Framework: a function it doesn't have) is never decided, so with the LLM gateway it reads as a live `reconcile.hook_missing`, whose detail says the call ran with no hook gating it, though it never ran. | Pydantic AI validates the arguments and finds the tool before `wrap_tool_execute`; Agent Framework answers an unknown function before function middleware. The gateway then sees a result (the retry prompt, the error) sent back for a tool use no decide covers. | Read such a `hook_missing` with the model's next request: what it sends for that id is the framework's retry prompt or error, not a tool's output. | [Pydantic AI](integrations/pydantic-ai.md#not-covered) |
| Microsoft Agent Framework: the signer decides the arguments Agent Framework validated from the model's JSON (values in the parameters' types, keys the function doesn't take dropped), not the model's JSON text, labelled `args_source: coerced`, so reconcile doesn't compare them with the model's tool use as the agent reports it; it does compare them with the LLM gateway's record of it (the label is the agent's word), where a converted or dropped value reads as `reconcile.args_mismatch` "(the decide says a framework coerced them)". A `SecretStr` argument is decided as `**********`. A middleware listed after `Tracekit` that rewrites them runs the call with arguments the signer never decided (recorded as `status: error`). | Function middleware is handed the validated arguments, never the model's raw call, and runs in list order. | Read a decision's arguments as what the function ran with; list argument-repair middleware before `Tracekit`; behind the LLM gateway, read such an `args_mismatch` against the gateway's record of the call. | [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered) |
| Microsoft Agent Framework's own tool approvals (`approval_mode="always_require"`) are not in the bundle, and a call its approver rejects leaves no record. | It holds the call for its approver before any function middleware runs; `Tracekit` decides the call once it is approved and replayed. | Use the policy's `ask` for approvals the bundle should hold. | [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered) |
| Microsoft Agent Framework: not decided are tools the provider runs (hosted MCP, web and file search, code interpreter), calls it hands back to the caller (`declaration_only`, `additional_tools`), the calls of a delegate (`agent.as_tool()`) or workflow agent that doesn't have the middleware itself, and middleware given to `workflow.as_agent(...)`, which it never runs. | Only calls Agent Framework runs through the function middleware of the agent, run or chat client running them reach `Tracekit`. | Give every agent `Tracekit(run)`, or put it on the chat client they share; hosted calls are seen only in the model responses the [gateway](gateway.md) records. | [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered) |
| Microsoft Agent Framework: tools a CodeAct provider (`agent-framework-monty`, `agent-framework-hyperlight`) exposes inside the code it runs are not decided; only the `execute_code` call and its code are. | The provider calls them through `FunctionTool.invoke` from the generated code, outside function middleware. | Give a tool that must be gated to the agent directly, not to a CodeAct provider. | [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered) |
| Microsoft Agent Framework: a refusal reaches the model as the call's result text, not as Agent Framework's error result, so its error limit doesn't count it. | Middleware that doesn't call the next one can only set the result; an exception would show the model only `Error: Function failed.`. | Nothing: the text starts `Tool call blocked by Tracekit`, `Tool call held by Tracekit` or `Tool call refused`. | [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered) |
| Microsoft Agent Framework: `Tracekit` is tested with Agent Framework 1.22 only and `tracekit.instrument()` doesn't add it; another version warns when you build `Tracekit(run)`. An `ask` blocks the call, holding a thread of asyncio's default executor, so many asks at once can starve it. | The middleware is built by hand and calls the signer through the synchronous client. | Install `agent-framework-core>=1.22,<1.23` and build `Tracekit(run)` yourself; answer approvals promptly. | [Microsoft Agent Framework](integrations/microsoft-agent-framework.md#not-covered) |
| Google ADK: a call ADK pauses for its own tool confirmation or for credentials is decided again when ADK runs it again (its next attempt; the first reads `decided_not_completed`), so a tool under both a Tracekit `ask` and ADK's confirmation is approved in Tracekit twice. A paused call another process resumes is decided as attempt 0 again, and refused under an `ask`. | ADK runs a paused call again under the same call id; the plugin counts attempts in memory, and ADK's confirmation is not a signer approval. | Use Tracekit's `ask`, not ADK's confirmation, for an approval in the evidence; resume a paused session in the process that paused it. | [Google ADK](integrations/google-adk.md#not-covered) |
| Google ADK: handing a task to a task-mode sub-agent (`LlmAgent(mode="task")`) is not decided, only the sub-agent's own tool calls are. The agent's own `before_tool_callback` runs after the decision: arguments it rewrites run undecided (recorded as an error, "the call's arguments changed after the decision") and an answer it returns instead of the tool is recorded as `ok`, as if the tool ran. `ComputerUseTool` rescales its coordinates in place, so its coordinate actions read as changed after the decision. A tool confirmation's `payload` reaches the tool undecided. | ADK runs a task-mode sub-agent outside the tool callbacks (an answer from `before_agent_callback` would leave the hand-off unanswered and the session stuck with that sub-agent), runs the agent's callbacks after every plugin's, and hands the payload to the tool directly. | Gate what the sub-agent does through its tools, or hand over with `transfer_to_agent` or `AgentTool`, which are decided; keep argument rewrites and canned answers in the tool, not the agent's `before_tool_callback`; read computer-use "arguments changed" records as its rescaling; carry nothing policy must see in a confirmation's `payload`. | [Google ADK](integrations/google-adk.md#not-covered) |
| Google ADK: code an agent's `code_executor` runs, builtin tools the model provider runs, and the tools of an agent run as `AgentTool(include_plugins=False)` are not decided. A long-running tool, or a streaming tool in a live run, is recorded with the pending status it returned, not its final result. | They never reach the Runner's plugin callbacks, or finish after them. | Give side effects to function or MCP tools and leave `include_plugins` on; read a long-running tool's final result in the ADK session next to the bundle. | [Google ADK](integrations/google-adk.md#not-covered) |
| Google ADK: a plugin listed before `Tracekit` can change a call's arguments before the decision, answer its error (recorded as `ok`) or replace its result (`decided_not_completed`). A result a later `after_tool_callback` replaces is recorded as the tool returned it, and a tool that changes its own arguments in place reads as changed after the decision. | ADK runs plugin callbacks in the order listed and stops at the first that answers; Tracekit sees only its own place in that chain. | List `Tracekit` first in `plugins` (a warning says so when it isn't); change results and arguments in the tool itself. | [Google ADK](integrations/google-adk.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: with `raise_on_tool_invocation_failure=True`, a tool that raises ends the agent run before `after_tool`, so the calls of its step read as `decided_not_completed`; so does a tool output that fails to merge into the agent's state (`outputs_to_state`), whatever that setting. | The `Tracekit` hook records outcomes at `after_tool`; Haystack raises past it. | Keep the default `raise_on_tool_invocation_failure=False`: the model reads the error and the call is recorded as one; give `outputs_to_state` handlers that don't raise. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: an `after_tool` hook listed before `Tracekit` that drops a tool result (or a whole step) leaves that call `decided_not_completed`. | The hook records the results it finds after the step's calls; a dropped one is gone. | Under `after_tool`, list `Tracekit` ahead of a hook that drops results, or keep that hook from dropping the step's own. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: tools the model provider runs itself (`OpenAIResponsesChatGenerator`'s built-in `web_search`, a remote `mcp` server) are not gated. | They run on the provider's side and never reach the Agent's hooks. | Read them in the model responses autotrace records under `instrument()`, or give the agent Haystack tools instead. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: a value a tool writes to the agent's state (`outputs_to_state`) and a later tool reads from it (`inputs_from_state`, a `State` parameter) is not decided, so `from: untrusted` rules don't see an untrusted tool's values passed that way. | Only the model's arguments are decided; Haystack adds state values after the hook. | Don't pass an untrusted tool's output to another tool through state; let the model pass it as an argument, where it is decided. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: a `before_tool` hook listed after `Tracekit` can add a call, which runs undecided; a call it changes runs, recorded as an error. | Haystack runs the calls of the last message once every `before_tool` hook has returned. | List `Tracekit` last under `before_tool` and `after_tool`. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: an `ask` holds its whole step, so the step's allowed calls wait for the answer too (up to 5 minutes). | The hook decides a step's calls at `before_tool`, before Haystack runs any of them. | Answer approvals promptly; tools that often need one can be given to an agent of their own. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: a refused call's error result is not streamed (`streaming_callback`) or counted (`tool_call_counts`); an agent holding the hook can't be serialized (`SerializationError`); with `instrument()`, an `mcp-haystack` tool is decided twice. | Haystack streams and counts only the calls it runs; the hook holds a live run; `instrument()` wires MCP's `ClientSession` on its own. | Read refusals in the messages; build agents in code; don't call `instrument()` in a process whose Haystack agents use MCP tools. | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Haystack: a call without an id is decided under `haystack-<hex>`, which the hook writes into the history, so its result is checked under that id; it pairs with the LLM gateway's tool use only by its arguments (`paired_by_args`), never when two calls of a turn are alike. | The model's response gave the call no id, so the gateway records it under one of its own. | Use a chat generator that returns the model's tool-call ids (OpenAI, Anthropic). | [Haystack](integrations/haystack.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Strands Agents: an `ask` waits at most 5 minutes in the `BeforeToolCallEvent` hook, not in a Strands interrupt; a call an interrupt stops after Tracekit decided it (another hook's `event.interrupt`, the tool's own `ToolContext.interrupt` or its sub-agent's, which also re-runs the tool from the start) is decided again when the agent resumes, and an `ask` asks a person again. | An interrupt's response comes from the app, not from an approver the signer knows; Tracekit decides before other hooks and the tool run. One approval wait is capped at the signer's 300 s. | Answer the approvals of Strands calls within 5 minutes, or the call is refused and the model told; gate a call with a Tracekit `ask` or with an interrupt, not both. | [Strands](integrations/strands.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Strands Agents: a hook that runs after Tracekit's last check (at `math.inf`, added after it) isn't stopped: a call it changes runs, recorded as `status: error`; a refusal it clears runs, signed as `executed_against_policy`; one that also changes the call's `toolUseId` leaves it uncompleted (`decided_not_completed` if it was allowed, no record if it was refused). Strands' internal tool middleware can replace a call unseen. | Tracekit's last `BeforeToolCallEvent` callback runs at `math.inf`; a hook added after it at the same order runs later, and nothing between the hooks and the tool can be checked. | Don't add hooks at `math.inf`; read those records as a hook's doing, not the model's. | [Strands](integrations/strands.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** Strands Agents: arguments that aren't JSON are decided as `{}`; with `background_tasks`, a background tool another hook asks to retry runs again neither decided nor recorded; a result of several content blocks doesn't match the gateway's verified tool results. | Strands parses the model's arguments before any hook (`{}` is what the tool gets); a background retry fires no `BeforeToolCallEvent`; the gateway matches one form of a result, and Strands joins blocks differently per provider. | Read `{}` arguments as "the model's text didn't parse"; don't set `retry` for background tools; return one text or `json` block from a tool whose results you verify. | [Strands](integrations/strands.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** LlamaIndex: only the object given to `Tracekit(run).gate()` is gated. An `AgentWorkflow` runs every agent's tools in its own `call_tool` step, so agents gated alone gate nothing there; tools called outside an agent workflow's step (`llm.predict_and_call`, router or sub-question query engines) run ungated, and the functions `CodeActAgent`'s code calls inside `code_execute_fn` aren't decided one by one. While a gated call runs, `instrument()`'s MCP session decides nothing made inside it, so an ungated agent that a tool runs has its MCP calls undecided. | The gate overrides the `call_tool` step of what it gates; `CodeActAgent` runs the model's code as one `execute` call; a call the gate decided must not be decided again by the session it runs through. | Gate the object you `.run()` (the workflow, else the agent) and every agent a tool runs; run `CodeActAgent`'s code in a sandbox. | [LlamaIndex](integrations/llamaindex.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** LlamaIndex: `ReActAgent` and `CodeActAgent` calls are decided under an id LlamaIndex makes, so with model calls recorded (the LLM gateway, autotrace) each reads as `reconcile.fabricated`. A call the model integration names by its tool (Ollama's, Google's) or gives no id is decided under a UUID of the gate's own: it pairs with the model's tool use only by its arguments (`paired_by_args`), never when two calls in one turn are alike, and it gets a new UUID, not a next attempt, when it runs again. A tool that waits for an event (`ctx.wait_for_event`) runs again from its start and is decided again as its next attempt: an `ask` is asked again, and the first attempt reads as `decided_not_completed`; a process that resumes a saved `Context` counts attempts from 0 again. | Their model writes an action as text, with no tool-use id; an id that names the tool would make two calls of it one; LlamaIndex runs a step that waited for an event again from the start, and the attempt count lives in the `Tracekit` object. | Use a `FunctionAgent` with a function-calling model where reconciliation matters; in a tool that waits for an event, do its side effects after the wait. | [LlamaIndex](integrations/llamaindex.md#not-covered) |
| **(1.3)** LlamaIndex: a gated object is bound to its `Tracekit`'s run for life (gating it again raises), and an untrusted tool's structure that can't be observed only warns, leaving the values the text's patterns don't find out of provenance. An `ask` holds its `call_tool` step for up to 5 minutes; a workflow `timeout` under that cancels the wait and withdraws the approval, and each wait holds a thread of asyncio's default executor. A `call_tool` your own subclass declares with `num_workers` or a `retry_policy` runs with LlamaIndex's defaults once gated, and an argument change in an override of LlamaIndex's private `_call_tool` is not seen. | The gated class holds its `Tracekit`, and the observe is one more request after the tool ran. The decision is made inside the step, before LlamaIndex's `call_tool` runs; the gated class declares that step once, with the defaults, and compares the arguments `call_tool` reports with the decided ones. | Build and gate the workflow inside each run (a server: per conversation); leave the workflow's `timeout` unset or above 300 s, answer approvals within 5 minutes, and keep fewer asks waiting at once than the executor has threads; don't rely on step options on a gated `call_tool`, and change arguments in a `call_tool` override, where the change is recorded as an error. | [LlamaIndex](integrations/llamaindex.md#not-covered) |
| Hermes Agent: an `ask` waits inside the plugin's `pre_tool_call`, which Hermes bounds by `plugins.hook_callback_timeout` (30 s by default, 600 s at most) and by the call's tool deadline (`timeouts.tools.sequential_call`, else `timeouts.tools.concurrent_batch`, 420 s by default; a concurrent batch's counts from the batch's start). A call not approved 5 s before the smaller ends is blocked and its approval withdrawn, so a hold lasts at most about 7 minutes by default and 10 with every limit at 600; so is a call Hermes stopped waiting for first. A hook that runs over its callback timeout (a signer that doesn't answer in time) is blocked by Hermes, which then skips it, and so blocks every tool call, for 60 s; a `post_tool_call` that runs over it (a signer slow to record an outcome) is skipped for 60 s too, so outcomes in that time aren't recorded and a call Hermes stops waiting for meanwhile isn't withdrawn from its hook. | Hermes bounds a plugin's callback so a hung one can't stall the agent, and blocks the call when `pre_tool_call` runs over: it fails closed. Past the tool deadline it tells the model the call timed out and stops waiting for it. | Set `plugins.hook_callback_timeout`, `timeouts.tools.sequential_call` and `timeouts.tools.concurrent_batch` to 600 and answer approvals within about 10 minutes; a call held longer is blocked, and the model told. | [Hermes Agent](integrations/hermes.md#not-covered) |
| Hermes Agent: tools an `execute_code` script calls are blocked, every one. Calls that fire no `pre_tool_call` aren't decided or recorded: another plugin's `ctx.dispatch_tool`, and every call in safe mode, which loads no plugins. A tool-execution middleware runs around the decision: one that answers in place of the tool leaves the call neither decided nor recorded, and a result it rewrites is recorded as rewritten. Another plugin's `modify` runs the call (recorded as an error, "the call's arguments changed after the decision"; Hermes's own repair of argument types isn't); a T2 approval of other arguments blocks the call. A session whose run can't be registered has every call blocked, whatever the fail mode. What the model said isn't recorded. | Hermes runs a script's tool calls with no `tool_call_id`, which a result can't be bound by; it calls the tool registry directly on the other paths, and runs middleware outside the hooks; `modify` merges over the arguments, so it can't run the signer's copy exactly; with no run there is no fail mode; the plugin uses only the tool hooks. | Have the model call tools itself rather than from `execute_code`; keep argument rewrites and canned answers out of other plugins and middleware in a gated Hermes; record the model calls with the [LLM gateway](gateway.md). | [Hermes Agent](integrations/hermes.md#not-covered) |
| A framework that catches exceptions around a tool or model call can turn a Tracekit refusal into an empty answer or a call that runs anyway (mcp-agent's `generate_str` returns `""` on a refused model call). | The refusal is an exception (`tracekit.Refused`) in the framework's control flow. | Let `tracekit.Refused` through your own error handling; see the per-framework notes. | [custom](quickstarts/custom.md#frameworks-that-catch-exceptions) |
| Every Browser Use `Tools` built after `instrument()` shares one signer stream, so the actions of all its agents are decided one at a time. | A run allows 64 streams, and each `Agent` builds its own `Tools`. | For one stream per agent, wrap each `Tools` with `trace_tools` by hand. | [Browser Use](integrations/browser-use.md#setup) |
| `tracekit.instrument(tracer)` (the v1 model-call tracer) is now `tracekit.autotrace.instrument(tracer)`. `tracekit.instrument()` takes the run's name and raises `TypeError` with that fix for anything else. | `tracekit.instrument()` is the one-line v2 entry point. | Call `tracekit.autotrace.instrument(tracer)`, and `tracekit.uninstrument()` to undo it. | [autotrace](quickstarts/custom.md) |
| Model calls are recorded only when made through the OpenAI, Anthropic or Google Gen AI Python SDKs. | Those are the clients autotrace wraps. | Record other calls with the client (`run.call("model_event", ...)`). | [custom](quickstarts/custom.md) |
| In TypeScript, `instrument()` returns the adapters for you to pass to the framework. It doesn't wire them. | ES modules can't be patched from outside. | `const tk = await instrument()`, then `tk.ai` (Vercel AI SDK) or `tk.agents` (OpenAI Agents JS). | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| In TypeScript, the run closes on `beforeExit`, and on SIGINT or SIGTERM when nothing else handles the signal (then the signal's default action runs), waiting at most 2 s for the signer. A signal your app or a library handles is left to it: the run closes at `beforeExit` if the process gets there, else it stays open until the idle timeout, as after `process.exit()` or an uncaught exception. `@openai/agents` installs SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers that exit the process. | Node runs no async work after `process.exit()`, and a handler's own shutdown must not be cut short. | In your own signal handler, `await tk.run.close()` before you exit. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** In TypeScript, a run with approvals still waiting for a person, or approved and not yet consumed, is left open when the process exits, for another process to approve and resume; the signer closes it after its idle timeout. | Closing it would expire the approvals before anyone answers them. | Save the run's id and token with the framework's state and re-attach in the resuming process: `new RunHandle(new Client(), { run_id, run_token })`. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md), [Vercel AI resume example](../sdk/typescript/examples/v2_vercel_ai_resume.mjs) |
| **(1.2)** In TypeScript, the adapters use the run made current with `withRun(run, fn)` (a run per conversation) for each call decided inside it, and the run they were built with otherwise; `instrument()`'s own run is replaced by a new one, linked as its `parent_run` while the old one is closing, when the signer closed it after it was idle. | A call's complete and approvals go to the run it was decided in. | A server makes each conversation's run current per request: `withRun(conversation, () => runner.run(...))`. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** OpenAI Agents JS: a sub-agent used as a tool (`agent.asTool()`) isn't gated, and without the LLM gateway its model responses aren't recorded, so its own gated calls reconcile as `fabricated`. | `asTool()` builds the tool itself; the nested run's responses aren't in the outer result's `rawResponses`. | Gate the sub-agent's own tools with `tk.agents.tool`, record the nested run's responses (`customOutputExtractor` gets its result), or run the LLM gateway. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** OpenAI Agents JS: with `toolExecution.preApprovalInputGuardrails` on, every `ask` is refused at once instead of interrupting the run (the approval stays requested until it expires). | Tracekit's guardrail consumes the approval; run before the interruption, it finds none granted. | Leave `preApprovalInputGuardrails` off. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** OpenAI Agents JS: a local MCP server's call that fails reaches no output guardrail, so `tk.agents.mcp(server)` leaves it decided and not completed (a `decided_not_completed` gap). | The SDK runs output guardrails only for a call that returned. | Read such a gap next to the MCP server's own log. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** OpenAI Agents JS with `mcpConfig.includeServerInToolNames`: `tk.agents.mcp(server)` turns the model's `mcp_<server>__<tool>` back into `mcp:<server>/<tool>` only when `server` is the MCP server's own `name`; a name the SDK shortened or hashed (long or colliding) is decided as given. | The SDK builds the prefixed name itself, from the server's name. | Pass the server's `name`; keep tool names short and distinct. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
| **(1.2)** Vercel AI: with `toolApprovalWith(app)`, a call your app answers `user-approval` and its person then denies in your app is left decided and not completed (a `decided_not_completed` gap); a call your app answers `denied` at once is recorded as refused. | The SDK tells the adapter nothing when your own approval UI says no. | Read such a gap as your app's refusal, or answer `denied` from the app function. | [TypeScript SDK](../sdk/typescript/README.md) |
