| **(1.2)** With the LLM gateway on the same run, model exchanges are recorded twice: the gateway's request and response, and the adapter's (`recordResponses`, the Vercel middleware). | Both record what they saw; the signer doesn't merge them. | With the gateway, the adapter's model events are optional; count exchanges by the gateway's (`source: gateway`). | [gateway](gateway.md) |

## `tracekit last`

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| It pins the dev signer's key from the signer's own data dir, so the verdict is `dev` assurance. | The dev signer runs as your user. Anything that runs as you could also have rewritten that key and the log. | For evidence against the agent, use system mode and witnesses, and pin keys you got another way. | [what dev assurance means](quickstart-v2.md#what-dev-assurance-means) |
| It shows finished runs only. A newer run that is still open is named and skipped. A run that just closed is waited for, up to 15 s. | The signer finalises a run a few seconds after the run closes. A bundle exported before that verifies only to its head. | End the agent, then run `tracekit last` again; or `tracekit last --open` (1.2.2) for the newest run as it stands, such as a coding agent's session, verified to its head. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#3-see-it-verified) |
| It writes `<run id>.tkb` and `<run id>.trust.json` in the current directory. | They sit where you can find them. | Use `-o PATH` to write somewhere else. | [quickstart](quickstart-v2.md#3-see-it-verified) |
| It reads only the same-user dev signer. | A configured signer's store and keys belong to another user or host. | `tracekit signer trust --config signer.yaml -o trust.json`, then `tracekit export --v2 --config signer.yaml --run ID`. | [deploy](deploy.md) |

## Platforms

| Limit | Why it is so | What to do | Details |
|---|---|---|---|
| Windows: dev mode only, with no caller attestation; v1 approvals are refused. | Python exposes no peer-credential or process-tree call there. | Point the witness at storage the agent's user can't rewrite. | [platforms](portability.md#windows) |
| macOS system mode is experimental and not validated on hardware. | `launchd` and user creation are unit-tested with mocks only. | Use `--experimental-macos` knowingly; Linux for production. | [platforms](portability.md#macos-system-mode) |
| Harness binding works on Linux system mode only. | It reads the process tree from `/proc`. | Use a separate user or a gateway elsewhere. | [platforms](portability.md) |
| The Claude Code plugin's hook script is POSIX shell. On native Windows Claude Code runs it in Git Bash (untested); without Git Bash, in PowerShell, which can't run it, so every tool call is blocked. Managed settings aren't wired up on Windows. | The wrapper is POSIX shell. | Without Git Bash, don't install the plugin: use `tracekit init --dev --v2`. | [plugin](../plugin/README.md#platforms), [platforms](portability.md) |
| On Windows, Codex, Gemini CLI and Qwen Code run Tracekit's hook through PowerShell (Codex through cmd.exe when PowerShell isn't found or the session has no single local environment), and a hook that can't start (its Python moved or was uninstalled) or crashes isn't wrapped to fail closed, so the call runs unrecorded; Qwen Code's `PreToolUse` hook is from 0.15.0 (it exits 2, so the call is blocked; before 0.15.0, the row on `tracekit init --agent qwen` on native Windows says what runs). With Python under a path with a space or any other character beyond ASCII letters, digits and `_ . : \ -` (a `/` too), the hook's command takes PowerShell's form, which cmd.exe can't run. There Codex's block is the JSON deny at exit 0, which output a cmd.exe AutoRun command prints first would spoil. Cursor's v2 pre-tool hook blocks the call instead (`failClosed`). Tested on Windows CI, which is advisory. | Windows has no POSIX shell to wrap the hook in, and cmd.exe and PowerShell read a quoted program path differently. | After moving or reinstalling Python, run `tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent codex` (or `gemini`, `qwen`) again; install Python under a plain path (`C:\Python312`); keep any cmd.exe AutoRun command silent. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md), [platforms](portability.md#windows) |
| On Windows, with Python under a path that holds a `$` or a backtick (`C:\Users\$dev\...`), the hook command `tracekit init` writes for Codex, Gemini CLI, Qwen Code and Antigravity CLI (PowerShell's `& "..."` form), for Claude Code (whose hooks run in Git Bash) and for goose (run with `sh -c`) can't start: Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI and agy then run each call unrecorded, and goose and Qwen Code from 0.15.0 block each one (before 0.15.0, see the row on `tracekit init --agent qwen` on native Windows). The Claude Code hook's approve and reject commands show a `TRACEKIT_SIGNER` that holds a quote, `$`, a backtick, a `%` or a line break as `<TRACEKIT_SIGNER>`, for you to fill in. | `tracekit init` writes the Python path in double quotes for every Windows harness, the quoting cmd.exe reads too (Codex falls back to cmd.exe), and the hook prints the signer in them for a person who may paste into either shell; PowerShell and `sh` (Git Bash's included) still expand `$` and backticks inside double quotes, and cmd.exe expands `%VAR%`. | Install Python, or make the virtualenv, under a path with no `$` or backtick, then run `tracekit init --dev --v2 --agent <agent>` again; check with `tracekit view --dev` that calls are recorded. | [coding agents](coding-agents.md), [platforms](portability.md#windows) |
| CI blocks merges on Linux only; macOS and Windows jobs are advisory. | Root-only and `/proc` tests need Linux. | Treat a green macOS or Windows run as advisory. | [platforms](portability.md#what-tested-means-here) |
| The committed performance numbers are from one macOS machine with `--quick`, file and Postgres stores; the latency gate is enforced on Linux only, and the Postgres run missed it (p99 5.17 ms). | Linux is where the gates run. | Run `make eval` or `eval/e9_signer_perf.py` on your own hardware. | [FAQ](faq.md#how-much-does-it-slow-an-agent-down) |
| Direct `Write`/`Edit` of recognized CI files (including composite actions and `azure-pipelines.yaml`) is held by `TK-A061` when a `curl`/`wget` command puts a variable or secret expression in an upload option's own argument (a JSON body with escaped or spliced quotes too), or pipes `echo`/`printf`/`cat` of a variable or an environment dump (`env`, `printenv`, `set`, `export`, `declare`), optionally through `base64`, to an upload from `@-` (or, for curl, `-T -` / `--upload-file -`) over HTTP(S). An authentication header, file upload or public `$GITHUB_SHA` alone is not an exfiltration. **Known false holds:** a deployment notice posting otherwise non-secret public CI metadata (`$GITHUB_REF_NAME`, `$GITHUB_RUN_ID`, `${{ env.X }}`, `$CI_COMMIT_SHORT_SHA` or `$CIRCLE_SHA1`) in request data is still held; the current exception covers only `$GITHUB_SHA` and `${GITHUB_SHA…}`, and only in a text that names `GITHUB_SHA` no other way (an assignment, an `env:` key, even a comment makes it any variable). | The bounded line matcher sees later block-scalar lines; commands after `;`, `&`, `\|`, the command keys (`run:`, `script:`, `before_script:`, `after_script:`, `command:`, Azure's `bash:`, `pwsh:` and `powershell:`) and Jenkins' `sh`, `bat` and `powershell` steps; behind the wrappers `sudo`, `doas`, `env`, `time`, `nohup`, `command`, `exec`, `builtin`, `nice` and `timeout` with their options and numbers, assignments, shell keywords, `(`, `{`, `!` and `X=$(`; and absolute program paths, but is not a YAML or shell parser. It misses commands joined with `\` or folded `run: >` scalars; an option's argument before the program (`sudo -u root curl`) and other wrappers (`bash -c '…'`, `xargs`, a `$(…)` in another command's arguments: `echo "$(curl …)"`); stdin from a here-string or file (`curl -d @- … <<< "$T"`); expressions other than `secrets`, `env` and `github.token` (a step output such as `steps.app.outputs.token`); `&`, `;`, `\|` or `#` inside data; scheme-less or variable URLs; an `Edit` whose `new_string` is only a URL or only `-d "$T"`; local-file `-T`/`--upload-file` or `--post-file` paths (their arguments name a local file; secret file contents are not inspected); patch-style edits, `MultiEdit` and workflows generated by scripts; leaks through a GET query or header, or actions that send secrets; ordinary `nc` uploads; and CI systems and paths it does not read (e.g. Bitbucket and `.gitlab/ci/` includes). Oversized CI writes are handled by the pack’s `TK-OVERSIZE` rule independently of this content matcher; CI deletions remain held. | Review CI changes and restrict secrets and network access at the runner; do not rely on this tripwire for full egress detection. | [policy](policy-v2.md) |
| E21's policy latency gate holds each decision's p99 as a multiple of a fixed reference workload timed right after it (`p99_ref`), within its recorded cap (25% over the recorded run's; for strict and dev, 5% over the runs of 2026-11-08), and only on the machine that recorded it: on CI's Linux runner, or any other machine, only CI's own check that p99 stays under 1 ms applies. A slowdown under the cap passes, and on the recording laptop every core busy moved `p99_ref` by up to 10%, its efficiency cores alone by up to 14%, which eats into that margin. | The reference cancels the machine's speed, not every difference between it and a decision (their caches differ); the ratio differs between machines and engine builds. | When decisions get slower on purpose (more rules), record the gates again (`eval/e21_policy_robustness.py --record`) and say why in the pull request; to gate on another machine, record there first. | [evaluation](evaluation.md#e21-policy-robustness) |
| Not measured: a separate-user signer's throughput, the HTTPS transport, cross-host throughput, adversarial-model bypasses, Windows and macOS system mode, an external review. | Not built or not run yet. | Measure what you depend on. | [evaluation](evaluation.md#what-is-not-measured) |
| The LangChain, MCP and OpenAI Agents adapters need Python 3.10 or newer. | Their frameworks require it. | Use Python 3.10+ for those adapters. | [platforms](portability.md#python-versions), [MCP](integrations/mcp.md#install) |
| The threat models haven't been reviewed by anyone outside the project yet. | Issue #1 tracks the review. | Read the [review packet](review-packet.md). | [laptop threat model](threat-model-laptop.md#open-problems) |
